## R-S2-58 - obj_018: THE REBOX REGRESSION — a user-accepted box lost to a re-run with a one-name ballot (2026-08-09)
- **USER THREAD** (cone_map review): "is this the latest?" — yes: cone_map.html = vote run 17 (08-08 20:49), and for obj_018 the page's perp render IS the measurement today's shipping box came from. Then: "i thought there was a time where the box reboxed to just the round dot light" — **CONFIRMED from the log.**
- **THE TIMELINE.** Run-14 chain (08-08 afternoon, USER-ACCEPTED): J8 shipped the rejected rebox candidate — 1.25x0.03x0.52 -> **0.17x0.05x0.16**, a 97% volume cut, the first J8 verdict ever to change a box. Run 17 (08-08 evening): the vote re-ran from `resolved`, the perp rebox re-detected, the judge chain re-ran UNGATED — and today's grouped box is the run-17 rebox `final_box` VERBATIM (**1.214x0.034x0.541**, byte-equal to the `rebox_truncated` doubt's final_box).
- **TWO STACKED FAULTS:** (1) the run-17 detection grabbed the round light + the lit strip as ONE object (score 0.287) — the day before the same step found the small light alone, so this is detector flakiness on a hard target (dim fixture beside glowing strips); (2) that grab ran off the photo on 2 of 4 sides, and the truncation rule filled those sides from the PRIOR — the original oversized lift box. Most of the 1.21 m is prior, not measurement.
- **THE MECHANISM THAT MADE IT SILENT:** the two rebox doubt kinds behave differently. `rebox_rejected_smaller` PRESERVES the measurement as a ballot candidate (J8 can ship it — run 14 did). `rebox_truncated` APPLIES its final_box and records NO alternative — run-17 J8's obj_018 ballot held exactly one key, `current`; ship=current was the only legal answer (settle_log: changed=false). A user-accepted box regressed, and the judge that once fixed it had nothing to choose.
- **J9 caught it from the product side** ("its huge measured size is flagged rebox_truncated so it is a bad box on the same small fixture"; canonical 0.177x0.035x0.160) — but J9 annotates and never resizes, so both statements sit in the graph and compose would build the 1.21 m light.
- **THREE CHANCES THE PERP SHOT NEVER HAD** (the plain-English framing the user asked for): **no retry** (one photo, one detection, accepted even at 0.29 confidence and clipped), **no memory** (each run's measurement REPLACES the last; run-14's correct answer is unreachable), **no challenge** (a one-name ballot).
- **USER: "lets keep this for next session."** Nothing designed or built. Direction candidates on record, all provenance-shaped rather than thresholds: a truncated rebox leaves the box it replaced ON THE BALLOT; the fourth-condition idea from R-S2-55 (a box a later judge disbelieved never feeds evidence); node_views' fresh aimed shots as the re-measure substrate.

## R-S2-59 - obj_018: THE RACE REPLAYED — the small light WAS a candidate; the combo lost it on prior match (2026-08-09)
- **THE QUESTION** (user): did the detector straight up not offer the small box in run 17? **ANSWERED: it offered it, at the HIGHEST confidence in the race.** Run 17 never cached its candidate list (gdino_best returns the full `choice` record; perp_rebox kept only the winner's score), so the detector was re-run on the CACHED run-17 render (params hash efe4f131f913, byte-identical, deterministic model = the same race). Prior rebuilt from the resolved box through the recorded camera; the winner's score reproduces the doubt record exactly (0.287).
- **THE RACE, two candidates:** #0 light+strip grab — det score 0.287, clipped 2 sides, prior match **0.602** -> combo 0.1209, **WINNER**. #1 the small round light — det score **0.534** (the detector's own top answer), clean, prior match **0.045** -> combo 0.0241, lost. The pre-08-08 ladder (untruncated tier first) would have picked #1; the run-14 chain proves the rest of the pipeline then goes right (sanity guard rejects the 3x shrink, `rebox_rejected_smaller` keeps it on the ballot, J8 ships it).
- **SO THE PROXIMATE CAUSE IS SCORING:** the combo's match term rewards covering the PRIOR — and the prior here is the oversized box the re-box exists to correct. When the prior is wrong-big, the formula systematically prefers wrong-big detections; obj_018 is the direct casualty of the obj_034 fix (truncation veto -> x0.7 discount). The provenance holes (one-name ballot, no retry) remain the reason it shipped SILENTLY — both fixes still undesigned. One-shot framing settled with the user: "memory of yesterday's answer" is a dev crutch and is NOT a fix; the one-shot-valid layers are scoring, ballot (never one name), retry.
- **ON THE CONE MAP:** obj_018's card now carries a red-bordered "DETECTION CANDIDATES — REPLAY 2026-08-09" panel — both boxes drawn on the photo, the score table, link to vote_obj_018_perp_candidates.json (+ _cands.png, pool_retake/slices/). Clearly labelled a REPLAY, not a run-17 artifact.
- **CODE (user: "lets cache those too"):** slicevote.py now records the full race as `det_choice` in the perp re-box doubt rec AND the card re-detect info (the top-view path already recorded; its "recorded ONLY here" comment corrected). Syntax-checked, uncommitted. Replay script in scratchpad: replay_obj018_perp_det.py.

## R-S2-60 - obj_018 SCORING FIX LANDED: DET_EDGE_PENALTY 0.7 -> 0.1, verified on every recorded race (2026-08-09, third session)
- **THE PATH TO IT, each step killed or kept by replaying the cached races** (all offline recomputes on recorded candidate lists — no detector runs, no re-renders): (1) **user's first idea, weight confidence x2** — a flat x2 is a no-op in a product (scales every candidate equally); the testable reading, score SQUARED, does NOT flip obj_018 (the 13x match gap dwarfs the 1.9x score edge) and DOES flip two verified-correct picks (obj_011 sofa, obj_026 pillow). Dead. (2) **drop the fill (coverage) term, match = containment only** — fixes obj_018 but hands obj_011 sofa to a score-0.466 fragment covering 26% of the prior (a cushion, fully inside — containment cannot reject a part of the true object). Fill's two roles (reward matching the prior's size = the poison; reject fragments = legitimate) are not separable by any threshold on fill itself: the cushion fills 26%, the true obj_018 light 2.3%, and no curve forgives one without the other. Dead, but the diagnosis stands.
- **THE RULING (user): penalize touching the edge.** The reasoning, in plain terms: a clipped box's extent is NOT a measurement, and in a re-box race the match term leans on a prior that is itself under correction — electing a clipped box that wins by filling a doubted prior compounds two unreliable measurements ("double wrong").
- **VERIFIED BEFORE LANDING, the sweep over every recorded race:** all 22 top-view winners are clear of the border — NO value of the penalty in [0, 0.7] flips any of them. obj_018's perp race flips to the detector's clean top answer (the small light) at any value <= 0.13.
- **THE obj_034 QUESTION SETTLED BY REPLAY.** The 2026-08-08 softening (veto -> x0.7) was obj_034's fix, so hardening the penalty risked trading obj_018 for obj_034 — and obj_034's race was never recorded. Replayed on the cached render (params hash 8910c9bc9a6f, deterministic model, winner reproduces the comment's 0.619/0.378 exactly): **NEITHER candidate touches the edge.** obj_034 was fixed by the score factor in the combo, not by the softness of the edge discount; the penalty value cannot touch its race. Recorded permanently: vote_obj_034_perp_candidates.json + _cands.png (pool_retake/slices/).
- **LANDED:** DET_EDGE_PENALTY 0.7 -> 0.1 in slicevote.py, with the full evidence trail in the constant's comment. Still a discount, not a veto — when every candidate is clipped the factor cancels and the ranking is unchanged. Syntax-checked, uncommitted. NOT re-run: the vote has not been re-executed; today's graph still carries run 17's obj_018 box.
- **ON RECORD, the honest caveat:** the edge flag is a PROXY. obj_018's grab is wrong because it fills an oversized prior, not because it touches the border; a future wrong-big grab sitting fully in frame pays no penalty. This fix is empirically justified on every race we have, not principled — the ballot fix (a truncated rebox records its alternatives) and retry remain undesigned and are still wanted (R-S2-58/59).
- **CONE MAP GREW THE EVIDENCE VIEW** (user: "can i see all the scores / the competing boxes"): every one of the 22 recorded top-view races now has a table (score / contain / cover / match / edge / combo, winner highlighted) AND an ALL-CANDIDATES image — every recorded box drawn on the cached shot the race ran on, #k labels matching the table. Pure drawings on cached pngs (vote_obj_XXX_top_cands.png), injected by regenerable scratchpad scripts (inject_race_tables.py, draw_race_boxes.py, replay_obj034_perp_det.py). The prior rectangle is NOT drawn — its pixels were never recorded for top views, only the overlap fractions.

## R-S2-61 - obj_001 DOOR: the box-room assumption's first full casualty; polygonal shell v2 DESIGNED (2026-08-09, third session)
- **THE CONTRACT under review** (vote stage, wall exemption + shell clip): gets the resolved box + the 4-wall shell; decides whether a box is wall-exempt and what ships; a mistake ships a box on a wall the observation never touched.
- **WHAT THE RECORD SHOWS.** obj_001's lift box is a thin-X slab (0.14 x 2.14 x 1.05 m) at z -6.09..-5.05 — ENTIRELY beyond the ZLO wall (z -3.64), 1.4-2.4 m outside the shell. The wall exemption ruled kept_wall vs ZLO at protrusion 0.0: distance BEYOND the plane is deliberately ignored (the obj_034 opening ruling), so fully-outside reads as perfectly flush and 0.0 protrusion WINS the least-protruding pick; nothing checks orientation (a sideways-facing door claimed by a z-facing wall). Perp re-box stood outside the shell (eye_outside_shell true), no detection. shell_clip then dragged z +2.45 m onto the plane -> shipped 0.043 x 2.142 x 0.020 m — a 4 cm door sliver. The doubt text "resolved box kept verbatim" is contradicted by the clip record beside it.
- **NO SINGLE STEP IS WRONG** — dots are real (Marble generates past openings), shell is measured + collider-agreed, ignore-beyond is a deliberate ruling, clip is by design. THE GAP IS COMPOSITIONAL: the pipeline has no representation for "beyond the shell" as a legitimate place, so the v1 box-room forces everything outside onto the nearest crossing plane.
- **AUDIT RUN ON living_marble** (room_shell.py --audit, first time on this scene): x_high / z_high razor sharp (peak widths 4 cm, collider agrees to 6-9 mm). x_low / z_low MUSHY: strongest peaks 0.5-1.7 m BEYOND the fitted v1 walls (widths 0.76 / 0.91 m) — wall material past both walls, matching the user's description (the x_high wall runs past the z_low wall; an arbitrary-angle connector joins them). Collider: 63 of 215 m^2 oblique. Artifacts: out/living_marble/room_shell_audit.json + .png — USER REVIEW of the occupancy image pending.
- **W4 DESIGNED (user's algorithm, PLAN_ROOM_SHELL.md §3-W4): TRACE what you can → CLOSE the polygon (added segments MARKED inferred) → MERGE similar planes (cardinal snap = a merge special case; connectors keep measured angle, their ENDPOINTS absorb closure error — walls never move; topology first, then geometry).** Mesh = VALIDATION ONLY (user). Scope pin: connectors are the only non-cardinal geometry; angled walls out (axis-aligned boxes + the 2 cm compose lattice). NOT BUILT — awaiting go.
- **VOCAB CORRECTION (user, again):** "carve" — retired 08-08 — was echoed back from run-17 DATA (doubt texts / report / cone map predate the rename and keep the old word until a re-run). Two code stragglers renamed (view_cams.py docstring, serve.py HUD text); memory updated: when quoting old-run data, TRANSLATE.
- **QUEUED BEHIND THIS:** the card-race audit (~100 cached-render replay detections to certify the DET_EDGE_PENALTY change can't shift any card vote; user approved, deferred until after obj_001), then the J9 gate (SIXTH session open).

## R-S2-62 - W4 POLY SHELL BUILT: trace->close->merge runs on living_marble; four rules earned on four failures (2026-08-09, third session)
- **CONTRACT:** `room_shell.py --poly` gets the splat + frame block; decides the room OUTLINE as N segments (cardinal walls on majority planes, connectors at traced angles, everything marked measured/inferred); a mistake draws the room where it is not. REVIEW ARTIFACTS ONLY — `room_shell_poly.json` + `.png`, no consumers until this gate passes.
- **RESULT (after iteration):** 9 segments, 8 measured / 1 inferred — the room rectangle + the SW pocket beyond z_low reached through the doorway at the south wall's west end, closed by angled connectors (the user-predicted "east wall past south wall" connective geometry, at its measured angle). Dresser bump and corner-shelf notch eliminated by the min-wall rule.
- **THE FOUR RULES, each bought with a visible failure on this scene:** (1) solid = dense WALL-BAND material, not v1's reaches-the-ceiling — living_marble's ceiling splat is DENSE, so every open-floor cell "reached the ceiling" and the whole room read as solid (v1's hidden assumption, now on record); (2) TALL rule — solid must reach 1.4 m above floor, else the sofa welds to the wall band and bites a 2 m dent; (3) FLOOR rule OUTSIDE the box only — floor-level splat separates a walk-in pocket from the view through a window (kills the band past the west curtains that out-sized the room and defeated a geodesic cap); INSIDE the box it would dig fake dents where furniture shadows the floor; (4) MIN WALL GROUP 2.0 m (user ruling: "segment 04 is actually a shelf at the corner") — a cardinal group under 2 m total is furniture standing against architecture; dropped, neighbours close straight across. Judged per GROUP so a short fragment of a long wall survives.
- **MECHANICS:** Moore-neighbour boundary trace (plt.contour fragments a jagged mask into open pieces — replaced), closed-loop Douglas-Peucker (plain DP degenerates on a closed loop), majority plane = length-weighted vote inside each wall group, square steps between parallel planes, connectors >= 0.5 m keep their angle, reach cap 3 m bounds pocket depth.
- **OPEN AT THE GATE:** (a) shelf corner now a CHAMFER (measured connector along the shelf face) — vs extending the two walls to a square corner; (b) cosmetic: west wall = 2 records on one plane (zero-length joint dropped between them); (c) obj_001's door corner (x +2.7, beyond z_low east side) is OUTSIDE the polygon — "beyond the shell" handling is a consumer decision, not a tracing one; (d) consumer wiring (vote-stage wall walk, shell_clip against the outline, perp cameras per segment) NOT started.
- **QUEUED BEHIND:** card-race audit (~100 cached replays, user-approved), J9 gate (SIXTH session).

## R-S2-62b - THE 2 m BAR UNIFIED: connector chains judged like walls; polygon reaches 7 segments (2026-08-09, third session)
- **USER (on the new overlay): "the 04 connector is also simply an artifact of the shelve. i think the length limit can be even higher."** Raising the per-connector limit would have amputated the pocket (its pieces are 0.78-1.2 m each); the ruling is implemented at CHAIN level instead: a run of consecutive connectors is an architectural feature only if it TOTALS POLY_WALL_MIN_M (2.0 m) — the same bar wall groups must clear. The shelf chamfer (1.0 m) drops and the north/east walls meet at their square corner; the pocket ramp (3.1 m chained) clears it.
- **RESULT: 7 segments, 6 measured / 1 inferred** — four walls (west healed into ONE 11.9 m record; both R-S2-62 nits gone without separate fixes) + the pocket's 3-connector chain. ONE principle now covers all demotions: every architectural feature — wall group or connector chain — earns 2 m of traced length or the walls close over it.
- Still open at the gate: consumer wiring; obj_001's corner outside the polygon ("beyond the shell" = consumer decision).

## R-S2-62c - ONE SEGMENT PER CHAIN: the polygon lands at 5 segments, all measured (2026-08-09, third session)
- **USER: "04, 05, 06 can be simplified. lets limit connectors to one segment."** A surviving connector chain now collapses to a single straight segment from the chain's start to its end (the wiggle is trace noise; the span is the architecture); endpoints re-anchor onto the neighbouring wall planes as before.
- **RESULT: 5 segments, 5 measured / 0 inferred** — west 12.3 m, north 5.3, east 9.0, south 4.3, and ONE 3.4 m diagonal connector closing the pocket (ink 0.76). This is the user's original design statement realized end to end: snap cardinal where possible, one arbitrary connective piece adjusts to fit.
- Final recipe on record: trace (band-density + tall + outside-box floor rules) -> close -> merge to majority planes -> 2 m architectural bar (groups AND chains) -> one segment per chain.

## R-S2-63 - W5 WIRED: the five shell consumers walk the polygon (2026-08-09, fourth session)
- **CONTRACT:** slicevote's shell handling gets the room outline; it decides which wall (if any) claims a box, what the shipped box is clipped to, which dots may vote, and where a camera may stand. A mistake ships a box on a wall it never touched (the obj_001 sliver) or lets a distant wall claim a box through an infinite plane.
- **RULINGS D1-D3 (user):** D1 = treat as before, but a box ENTIRELY outside the polygon is IGNORED (dropped_outside_shell row in the report + cone map, removed from the manifest by the merge, never silent). D2 = connector-claimed boxes get NO perp re-box (kept, recorded; connectors stay the only non-cardinal geometry). D3 = ONE contract file: --poly folds a "polygon" block into room_shell.json (raw-frame planes, interior sides, connector inward normal precomputed by the shell owner); the v1 rewrite prints a warning when it drops the block.
- **THE OBJ_001 QUESTION DISSOLVED BY MEASUREMENT:** against the FINAL 5-segment polygon the door is 55% inside (centre inside), a 6 cm overhang past the east wall - an ordinary wall-protrusion exemption (0.077 m) on the pocket-side wall. The R-S2-62 corner-outside note was true of the intermediate polygon only.
- **THE KEY MECHANICAL CHANGE:** a segment can only claim or clip a box whose footprint OVERLAPS its extent (+WALL_TOUCH slack). The infinite-plane claim/drag is gone on the polygon path; the v1 4-plane path is preserved verbatim behind `POLY is None` (a scene without a polygon block behaves byte-identically).
- **ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING (test finding, kept on purpose):** wall_protrusion CAN claim a fully-outside box - "beyond the plane reads as flush" is the deliberate obj_034 opening ruling - so the D1 drop runs FIRST in the per-node loop. The offline test documents this instead of "fixing" it.
- **VERIFIED OFFLINE (17/17, scratchpad test_w5_geometry.py, AST-extracts the real functions):** obj_001 claimed by wall_00 at 0.077 m, x clipped to the plane, z NOT dragged (the old path drags it - also tested, as documentation); fully-outside box detected; pocket box claimed by the connector at ~0.14 m; connector clip lands fully interior; point-in-polygon agrees with matplotlib on 5000 random points; in_bounds accepts the pocket and rejects beyond-the-line.
- **NOT RE-RUN:** the vote has not been executed; graph + preview manifest still carry run-17 boxes. PARAMS unchanged (no new tunables) - cached renders and all recorded races are untouched by construction.

## R-S2-65 - PRE-J9 UNATTENDED-RUN AUDIT: the polygon shell is production-wired (2026-08-10)
- **USER DIRECTIVE:** "double check and scan everything before J9 ... automated pipeline, no one manning it, a hundred scenes." Three parallel scans (hardcoded refs / print-crash landmines / graph-builder polygon readiness) + targeted fixes, everything compiled + the 20/20 geometry suite re-passed.
- **FIXED - crashes on any migrated scene (the audit's blocker class):**
  (1) five compose modules looked walls up by the LITERAL v1 ids (planes["arch_wall_x_low"]) -> KeyError on arch_wall_00..NN graphs. New shared arch_walls.py (wall_axis_planes + arch_label, id-agnostic); fit_check / fit_preview / rotation_check / propose_edits x2 / supported_by rewired.
  (2) propose_edits decided wall-slab AXIS by the id string ("_x_" in wall) - always false on W5 ids, every slab silently on the wrong axis; now reads the node's plane axis; connector-anchored slabs fail() honestly.
  (3) the kept shell dicts crashed on the CONNECTOR node (no value_raw) - now skip it.
  (4) migrate_walls_w5 wrote source "room_shell_poly" but every consumer selects source=="envelope" - a rederive would have silently lost ALL IN_WALL edges; tool fixed + the live living_marble graph patched (5 nodes).
- **FIXED - fresh-scene wiring (the 100-scene path):**
  (5) build_graph now builds arch nodes FROM THE POLYGON when the block exists (one node per segment, connectors included, source "envelope"); no block -> v1 path byte-identical.
  (6) build_edges/rederive wall claims via new wall_claim_dist: tangent-extent guard (+0.10 slack) + connector signed-distance branch; walls dict now passes full geometry. VERIFIED read-only on the live graph: 23/27 IN_WALL identical, the 4 diffs = judged edges the migration deliberately kept beyond fresh tolerance (obj_034/obj_035) + obj_017 corner tie (fresh=nearest, migrated=judged axis; both defensible).
  (7) room_shell.py default mode now RUNS the polygon fit itself - one command = the whole contract; fit failure = recorded polygon_error + v1 degradation, never a silent skip (the fold-order hazard is gone).
- **FIXED - unattended hygiene:**
  (8) stdout/stderr utf-8 reconfigure in paths.py (imported by every stage) - the print-crash class (8 hard cp1252 sites found, one already killed a run) is dead pipeline-wide.
  (9) --scene required=True in 17 stage scripts (12 graph/ + rotation_check + 4 intake) - a runner that forgets --scene now ERRORS instead of silently processing bedroom_marble; every existing caller verified to pass --scene first.
- **KNOWN-OPEN, recorded not fixed:** envelope.py placement legality is floor-points-only - inside the bbox but outside the polygon (adjacent room through a doorway) can pass; fix = rasterized in-polygon mask ANDed into check_placement (contained, speced). build_envelope_nodes fallback still emits a 3rd naming scheme (fires only with NO room_shell.json - the automated chain always runs room_shell). experiments/ keeps absolute paths + scene defaults (off the automated path). ~70 em-dash prints would crash only OEM codepages - neutralized by the reconfigure. Polygon arch nodes carry detector string "room_shell.py (W1)" (cosmetic).

## R-S2-66 - CHILDREN KEEP THEIR PHOTOS: SP4 enrichment children get evidence at mint time; living backfill (2026-08-10)
- **CONTRACT.** pano_recenter SP4 mints "enrichment children" — objects a close-up retake finds INSIDE a parent (magazines on a shelf). This decides what evidence a child node carries for the rest of its life. A mistake = a node born photo-less: no crop, no J6 description, a NO PHOTO row at J9, and no judge can ever check it against anything.
- **THE BUG (user surfaced it via the new J9 grounding page, asking where the odd obj_005 cards come from):** SP4 kept only the child's lifted 3D box; the retake view name survived but the 2D rect and the photo were dropped. lift_frame had the rect all along (`L["box"]`) — it was discarded one line later. Living casualties: obj_005_c00 / obj_017_c00, invisible since mint, "silently dropped then recovered" 08-13, still unjudgeable.
- **FIX AT SOURCE (scene-agnostic, no knobs):** children now carry `members_inline` — view + 2D rect + score + the image path stated per member (retake shots live in rc<sfx>/, outside the pano crop dir, so the member says where its photo is). build_graph turns inline members into ordinary evidence.members and cut_crops reads the stated path; downstream (J1/J6/J9/pick) needs zero special-casing. manifest_filter passes whole objects through, so the field survives the f30 filter.
- **UNATTENDED-RUN LOUDNESS:** build_graph now prints a counted warning listing every detection node with ZERO crops ("blind to J1/J6/J9") — the class can no longer be silent on a 100-scene run. describe_nodes' context_crop also honors the stated image path (it was silently falling back to the tight crop for any non-pano member).
- **BACKFILL ON living_marble (scratchpad backfill_child_crops.py, backups *.pre_child_crop_backfill.bak):** matched each child to its source detection by view+label+recorded score with ABORT on ambiguity (rc2_00 magazine 0.458 -> obj_005_c00 lower stack; rc2_03 magazine 0.505 -> obj_017_c00 upper stack — heights agree with the 3D boxes). Wrote members_inline into BOTH manifests (rc + f30, so a rebuild reproduces it), evidence.members + labels onto the two record nodes, crops cut via build_graph.cut_crops itself. J6 --appearance-only: 44 cache hits, exactly the 2 new described ("small stack of white magazines, blue-bordered cover" / "white and tan magazines standing upright, one blue-patterned cover"). J9 dry-run rebuilt: magazine pool 5/5 described, 5/5 with crops, the NO CROPS warning is gone from the sheet.
- **SECOND BUG UNCOVERED — USER RULED 2026-08-10: DOCUMENTED, SKIPPED ("relatively small").** Children of parents later ruled SAME OBJECT are never compared to EACH OTHER. Verified in the live graph: obj_005_c00 / obj_017_c00 have IoU 0.000 (0.39 m apart vertically), so SAME_CANDIDATE (floor IoU 0.40) can never nominate them and J1 never saw the pair — no edge of any kind exists between them in any layer. The escape route is lift noise: the same stack close-upped twice can land in two non-touching boxes, which is exactly the case an IoU nominator cannot catch. Harmless here (genuinely two stacks; J9's pool can now at least SEE both since they have photos), but on an unattended run a double-counted object ships twice with no record.
- **DESIGNED FIX, ON THE SHELF (build when it bites):** one nomination rule in build_edges.py, where all duplicate nomination lives — for each SAME_CANDIDATE pair (P,Q), any two same-LABEL detection nodes fully nested in P and Q respectively (the IN edges with frac_of_smaller ~1.0 already record this) get a SAME_CANDIDATE edge, zone "via_parents", NO IoU requirement (broken overlap is the failure being caught). J1 judges the pair from crops as usual — answerable at all only because of this session's photo fix. Deliberately NOT within a single parent (would nominate every pair of books on one shelf; SP4's mint-time IoU dedup covers that case). Cost on living: 1 pair, 1 J1 call. Companion nicety: build_graph should carry the manifest child's `parent` into provenance (today the parent is only recoverable by parsing "_c00" out of the id — the id-string disease, R-S2-65 item 2).
- **PROVISIONAL (Claude):** crops verified non-empty and shelf-level mechanically; the user has not yet judged the rebuilt sheets. NOT re-run: J9 full mode (the gate is still the user's).

## R-S2-67 - STALE CROP FILES: a rebuilt scene served dead objects' pictures; folders now wiped per run (2026-08-10)
- **CONTRACT.** graph/crops holds one picture per recorded detection; every crop-fed judge (J1 pairs, J9 same-product, pick) trusts that the file named obj_X_mNNN shows obj_X's detection NNN. A mistake shows a judge the wrong object's photo, and the judge's answer is about the wrong thing.
- **FOUND BY THE USER on the J9 sheet** ("where do these shots come from" — obj_005's row showed a coffee table, obj_013's showed magazines). Traced end to end: the detections were CORRECT (rectangles drawn on today's views land exactly on the shelf and the pillow; both lift to the right 3D spots). The FILES were wrong: 9 of 246 crops on disk (size census, obj_002/003/005/006/013/017/025/026/048) were cut with different rectangles than the graph records — obj_005_m000.png held what obj_006's box cuts (the coffee table), byte-for-byte the old object's photo.
- **ROOT CAUSE.** The 08-06 re-run (normalization day) re-detected the scene from the start and handed out ALL numbers fresh — old pool 232 entries / new 283; old obj_005 was the TABLE, old obj_013 a CHAIR. graph/crops was never emptied, and cut_crops SKIPPED any file that already existed ("content is a pure function of view image x box"). That assumption is false across re-runs: a leftover file from the old numbering shares a name with a new crop, and skipping serves the dead object's picture. 9 of 246 names collided; the rest cut fresh and were fine.
- **FIX (ownership, not checking — user rejected a size-check as not clean):** a stage owns its output folder; rebuilding means REPLACING, never topping up. build_graph now wipes graph/crops and cuts everything each run (--recrop flag gone, nothing to opt into; ~250 crops = seconds); describe_nodes wipes graph/crops_ctx the same way. No name checks, no size checks, no way for a leftover to be mistaken for current work.
- **LIVING REPAIRED** (scratchpad recut_all_crops.py, uses the fixed cut_crops on the LIVE graph — build_graph itself would overwrite the judged layers): all 246 re-cut, filenames identical so the graph needed no edit, census now 0 mismatches; obj_005_m000 shows the shelf, obj_013_m005 the maroon pillow.
- **WHO WAS ACTUALLY POISONED — narrower than first thought:** J6's describer reads the CONTEXT variants, which are cut fresh from view+box, so its descriptions were right all along (46/46 cache hits after the re-cut — nothing to re-describe). The wrong pictures reached only the judges reading the tight files: J9's sheet reasons ("mismatched coffee-table photo", "first photo is a mis-crop" — the judges noticed and coped) and any pair sheets built from graph/crops. Their caches key on sheet bytes, so affected pools re-decide on the fixed pictures next run.
- **PROVISIONAL (Claude):** bookshelf and pillow J9 pools will re-judge next run (their sheets changed); the user's J9 gate ruling should wait for that run's page.

## R-S2-68 - SAME PRODUCT IS A RELATIONSHIP; THE SIZE GOES INTO THE NODES (user ruling 2026-08-10)
- **THE TRIGGER (user, on the J9 door card):** "buy one at [0.077, 2.266, 0.971]" — a door 8 cm wide and 97 cm deep. Boxes follow the ROOM's axes; the same door on two perpendicular walls swaps width and thickness, and the exemplar's box was copied verbatim in room order. First reading called it a shopping bug; checking the fitter showed shopping already tries the asset turned 90° ("perm"), so the machine side was fine — then the user ruled the real principle:
- **USER RULING:** "same product is only a relationship. there is no sizing attached to it. the sizing is already embedded in the scene graph. we should fit the new size to the existing correct orientation." Plus two answers to direct questions: anchor = ALWAYS THE SUPPORT FACE; scope = ALL set members.
- **BUILT (materialize rule 5 is now a real edit, not an annotation):**
  - RELATIONSHIP: pairwise SAME_PRODUCT edges between set members (source judge_same_product), carrying the set id and the exemplar. No size on the edge.
  - SIZE INTO THE NODES: every member's box becomes the determined product size fitted to its OWN orientation (long floor side -> the member's longer floor axis) and anchored at its support face — ceiling-mounted keep their top on the ceiling (settled ATTACHED->arch_ceiling), wall-flush keep the wall face on the wall (settled IN_WALL, axis from the edge), everything else keeps its bottom; unanchored axes resize about center. Pre-resize box, exemplar, axis fit and anchors all in provenance. canonical_size as a node field is GONE — sizing lives in geometry.
  - ORDER: rule 5 now runs BEFORE edge re-derivation in the full pass (a resized box is a moved box; the edges follow the nodes). settle-only unchanged.
- **APPLIED ON living_marble:** 10 groups, 18 members resized, 36 SAME_PRODUCT edges, 0 conflicts, additive check PASS. The doors now agree in each one's own orientation (obj_000 0.97x2.27x0.08, obj_001 0.08x2.27x0.97). **obj_018's 1.26 m truncation artifact is REPAIRED through the set's agreement** (grouped = 0.199x0.031x0.139) — the set's product size did what the ballot/retry design (R-S2-58/59) was queued to do for this node; that design stays open for the general single-node case (voted/settled honestly keep the big box).
- **SHEET DISPLAY FIXED with it:** the card now prints "product size L wide x H tall x S thick, measured from <exemplar>; each member's box gets this size in its own orientation" instead of the raw room-order triple.
- **LIMIT ON RECORD:** orientation fitting handles axis-aligned members. A member whose box is wrong because the OBJECT stands at a diagonal (the chairs, R-S2-47 finding 3) is not fixed by this — the pipeline still never measures in-between rotations.

## R-S2-69 - THE GATE ESCALATES, NEVER DECIDES: the ballot fix built and proven on obj_018 (2026-08-10)
- **USER DESIGN PRINCIPLE (this session):** code is allowed to say "not on my authority"; it is not allowed to say "no". A guard that blocks a change must put the blocked choice in front of a judge or record it as an open question — never silently convert "I can't authorize this" into "the current box is correct".
- **BUILT (the R-S2-58/59 ballot, four files):**
  (1) slicevote.py perp_rebox records the RAW measurement (`rec["measured"]`, the percentile extents BEFORE the truncation guard refills clipped sides with priors).
  (2) record_vote_doubts.py: rebox_truncated gains `measured_candidate` (full box via the same rebox_proposed_box helper the rejected path uses). Old vote records without the field behave exactly as before.
  (3) judge_multiplicity.py: the candidate slot fills from EITHER doubt kind (rejected proposed_box wins if both exist); the truncated case gets its own honest "what" text ("the current box is part guess; this is what the view measured"); named_box resolves rebox_candidate from either source.
  (4) materialize_layers.py named_box mirrors the same lookup. NO new thresholds; PERP_MAX_RATIO untouched.
- **PROVEN LIVE ON obj_018** (partial re-vote, cached renders): with the R-S2-60 penalty the small light WON the election, the 3x guard rejected it, and the rejection doubt carried proposed_box [0.165 x 0.051 x 0.158] (score 0.534, 439 dots). J8's case offered ship keys [current, rebox_candidate] — **J8 shipped rebox_candidate at confidence 0.85** ("only one small round ceiling light fixture"), higher than it ever endorsed the big box (0.62 on the one-name ballot). Chain rebuilt: build_voted -> settle (j8_box_swap 0.17x0.05x0.16) -> J9 (all 7 lights one set, fresh call) -> grouped (product size 0.199x0.031x0.139). Additive checks PASS throughout. **The regression that took three sessions of forensics is closed by the judge choosing with its eyes.**
- **NOTE:** the truncated-path candidate (measured_candidate) compiled + guarded but was NOT exercised live — this run took the rejected path (the penalty fix changed which doubt fires for obj_018). It fires the next time any scene's re-box is accepted-but-truncated.
- **PROCESS BUG FOUND ON THE WAY, recorded:** record_vote_doubts.py without --apply updates graph/vote_doubts.json but NOT the graph's `vote` block, and J8 reads the BLOCK — my first J8 run silently judged the stale doubts. The guard at J8's entry catches a MISSING block, not a stale one. KNOWN-OPEN for the unattended chain: a freshness check (vote_doubts.json newer than the block = refuse) or make --apply the only mode.

## R-S2-73 - THE GATE BECOMES A MODULE: recrop_gate -> node_evidence (B1, decide only, 2026-08-10)
- **CONTRACT (what it gets / what it decides / what a mistake looks like).** GETS: the graph at layer `settled` (boxes final after J8 ship rulings, J8s cuts, J1 merges) plus every stored detection crop and its camera. DECIDES: for each node, which of its stored crops are still honest evidence, and what repair the rest need - `recut` (same photo, new rectangle), `reshoot` (a different view is required), `keep`, `blocked`. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: a node judged, named, described and BOUGHT on a picture of a different object - which is what has been happening, because nothing consumed the gate.
- **WHY IT EXISTS NOW.** The gate (which nodes show the wrong photo) and node_views (correctly aimed pictures) were BOTH WRITE-ONLY - grep found no code consumer for either. Meanwhile J9 and six other readers kept picking detector crops by detection score. The missing piece was never a better gate; it was a consumer. USER RULING: promote it to a module, because "each module enriches and edits the scenegraph to a better state".
- **USER RULINGS APPLIED:** (1) supersede per condition - borrowed / not_in_photo / escaped are pictures of the wrong thing and are dropped; only re-zoom keeps its crop ("mostly no is a no"). (2) ESCAPED is repaired by RE-CUT, not a render - the photo contains the box by definition. (3) GENEROUS MARGIN: re-cuts use describe_nodes' existing CONTEXT pads (0.35 side/top, 0.75 bottom, 40px floor), NOT CROP_PAD 0.10 - re-used rather than re-invented, so the margin stays a statement of meaning. The deeper bottom shows what the object sits on.
- **LAYER PINNED.** Was `scene_state.current(graph)`; the docstring called that `grouped` because grouped was current the day it was written. Once J9 consumes this module, "current" IS J9's output and the evidence would depend on the verdict it feeds. Now reads `settled` BY NAME and dies loudly if the chain has not reached it.
- **BUG FOUND AND FIXED (scene-agnostic).** The module resolved a member's photo and camera only under rig_sp0/crops, but a member may state its own `img` - build_graph.cut_crops has honoured that since inline retake members existed. The two SP4 enrichment children were reported "no usable crop" while photo AND sidecar sat in rig_sp0/rcc. Fixed via Scene.view_paths (same rule as cut_crops, no new convention). blocked 2 -> 0.
- **MY OWN PLAN WAS WRONG ONCE, corrected before shipping.** The supersede ruling is per CONDITION; the first cut applied it per NODE, which discards a node's good crop whenever it also owns a bad one. `keep_crops` is now decided per crop (a crop stays if clean, or if its only complaint is re-zoom) and the `keep+recut` repair kind was deleted - the keep is data, not a kind.
- **LIVING RESULT (nothing performed):** 45 nodes, 35 fire -> 24 recut, 11 reshoot, 10 keep, 0 blocked. Per-crop: 40 escaped, 17 escaped+not_in_photo, 14 clean, 9 not_in_photo+rezoomed, 6 not_in_photo.
- **HONEST NULL RESULT, on record:** ZERO crops fire `rezoomed` alone on this scene - every mis-zoomed crop also runs off the photo. The keep-the-re-zoom ruling is implemented but NOT EXERCISED here; living is evidence for neither reading. Do not cite it as validation.
- **LIMITS ON RECORD:** a re-cut fixes framing, NOT viewpoint, occlusion or resolution (zoom is capped by the original panorama pixels). And BOTH repairs inherit box error equally - a re-cut's rectangle comes from the projected CURRENT box, not from the detector, so a wrong box frames the wrong volume exactly as a render does. An earlier claim in conversation that re-cut was immune to this was WRONG. Box error is J8's problem, not J9's.
- **LOOK FOR (user review):** graph/node_evidence/index.html - do the 24 recut previews show the object with sensible surroundings (not cropped tight, bottom showing what it sits on)? Are the 11 reshoots genuinely un-fixable from their photos? Do the 10 keeps look right to leave alone?
- **NOT DONE:** B2 re-cut execution, B3 reshoot (queued, awaits go - never render before the user says go), B4 the `shown` layer, B5 wiring the seven readers, B6 the map. J9 re-judging on repaired evidence WILL change verdicts, including the chair split the scene is gated on.

## R-S2-74 - B2: THE RE-CUTS TAKEN (same photographs, new rectangles, no GPU - 2026-08-10)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: B1's repair plan. DOES: for every node planned `recut`, cut its source photograph again around the CURRENT box, padded with the context family, into graph/node_evidence/recut/. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: a re-cut framing the wrong volume, or a file that quietly replaced another node's picture.
- **EXECUTION IS BEHIND A FLAG.** `--recut`; without it the module still only decides. Re-cuts cost no GPU and call no model, but "performs" and "decides" should never be the same default.
- **OWNERSHIP RULE APPLIED FROM THE START:** recut/ is WIPED and rebuilt every run, never topped up (R-S2-67). graph/crops is not written to at all - verified untouched at 246 files before and after.
- **BUG CAUGHT BY COUNTING, NOT BY EYE:** the run reported 40 re-cuts written and 39 landed on disk. A node can hold TWO detections in ONE photograph, and naming files `<node>__<view>.png` made the second overwrite the first. Same failure class as the 08-06 stale crops: one filename, two writers. Fixed by carrying the detection into the name (the stored crop's stem, already unique per detection) rather than inventing a counter. Re-run: 40 written, 40 on disk.
- **LIVING RESULT:** 40 re-cuts for 24 nodes, 0 degenerate skips. Sizes 133x106 to 453x665; none under 60 px on a side, so the generous margin is not producing postage stamps.
- **THE PAGE NOW SHOWS THE REAL FILE.** Where a re-cut was actually taken the review page displays that file, not a redrawn preview with the box wire on it - the judge gets the clean picture, so the clean picture is what should be reviewed.
- **LOOK FOR (user review):** graph/node_evidence/index.html, the "what a re-cut would give" column, now labelled "the file itself". Is the object recognisable with its surroundings? Is the bottom margin showing what it sits on? Anything framing the wrong thing means the BOX is wrong, not the cut - that is J8's problem and should be reported as such.
- **STILL NOT DONE:** B3 reshoots (11 nodes, queued, awaits go - never render before the user says go), B4 the `shown` layer, B5 wiring the seven readers, B6 the map.

## R-S2-75 - B3: THE RESHOOTS COST NOTHING (the reuse gate paid for itself, 2026-08-10)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: B1's 11 `reshoot` nodes. DOES: give each one the aimed pictures that serve it, resolved to files that exist. RENDERS NOTHING - node_views.py owns the cameras and the GPU batch. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: a judge handed a filename with no picture behind it, or pictures framing a box the plan never decided on.
- **NO GPU WAS NEEDED AT ALL.** node_views planned 48 views over the 11 nodes and reported 0 pending: 22 kept existing node_views renders, 26 satisfied by REUSING prior shots that still frame today's box. The user's render go was not spent. This is R-S2-57's reuse gate paying for itself.
- **LAYER BUG FOUND BEFORE IT BIT (scene-agnostic fix).** node_views read `scene_state.current_name`, which is `grouped` - J9's OWN OUTPUT, and J9's SAME_PRODUCT rule writes product size INTO member boxes. On living, 3 of the 11 reshoot nodes (obj_014, obj_016, obj_011#1) have a DIFFERENT box in grouped than in settled, so aiming at current would have framed the verdict's box and handed it back as evidence for that verdict. node_views gained `--layer` (default unchanged); node_evidence passes `settled` and REFUSES to attach if node_views.json was aimed elsewhere.
- **A REUSED VIEW'S PICTURE IS NOT IN node_views/.** 26 of the 48 `file` entries do not exist there - the reuse verdict points at a prior shot in pool_retake/. Resolving only the obvious path would have attached 26 dead filenames, and nothing would have complained until a judge got a missing image. Both paths are now tried, and a view resolving to neither is reported by name.
- **LIVING RESULT:** 48 pictures attached, 0 problems. Every one of the 45 nodes now ends with evidence: 40 re-cuts (24 nodes) + 48 aimed pictures (11 nodes) + 13 surviving stored crops (10 nodes). Zero nodes with nothing.
- **LOOK FOR (user review):** the reuse rule is GEOMETRIC and blind to occlusion - its documented blind spot. "Frames the box correctly" is not "shows the object well". The 26 reused shots are the ones to spot-check for something standing in front of the object.
- **NOT DONE:** B4 the `shown` layer (write this evidence into the graph), B5 the seven readers, B6 the map.
- **PAGE BUG, USER-CAUGHT (obj_039, obj_042):** the review page showed reshoot nodes a preview of a RE-CUT - the repair those nodes were denied (their box runs off every photo they own). It advertised the rejected repair and hid the chosen one. Fixed: a reshoot node now shows the aimed picture actually serving it, labelled reused-prior vs rendered-for-this-box, with its view count. Column renamed "what a re-cut would give" -> "what it will be judged on instead". Verified: 0 previews left, 0 broken image links.
- **PATTERN WORTH WATCHING (twice now):** the page keeps flattering itself - first a redrawn preview instead of the real cut file, then a preview of a rejected repair. The rule that fixes both: show the file a judge will actually be handed, never a reconstruction of it.

## R-S2-76 - ONE FOLDER, TWO WRITERS: pool_retake split into vote/ and exp_pool_retake/ (USER ORDER, 2026-08-10)
- **USER ORDER:** "if it coming from two different methods we should not put it in the same place nor name it the same."
- **WHAT WAS WRONG.** `pool_retake/` held TWO unrelated collections under one name: slicevote.py's live vote output (slicevote_report.json, conemap*, slices/ 1023 files, rows/, the run*_canonical_backup sets) AND experiments/pool_retake.py's candidate renders + pool_targets.json. The folder is named after the EXPERIMENT, so anything found in it read as "the vote stage uses this". It had already cost a real data loss (R-S2-72: parallax_retake and pool_retake writing the same filename, one clobbering the other, the viewer showing mislabeled data) and it cost a wrong provenance call in conversation this session.
- **THE RULE APPLIED:** a folder has ONE owner and is named for the STAGE that owns it - the same ownership rule as R-S2-67, applied to directories instead of files.
- **THE SPLIT (living, the only scene with the folder):** 589 entries -> `vote/` 83 (slicevote's, live) + `exp_pool_retake/` 506 (the experiment's cards, _det overlays, iso variants, pool_targets.json). Sorted by an explicit filename rule, dry-run first, nothing ambiguous, `pool_retake/` removed empty.
- **CODE REWIRED:** vote side - build_voted, judge_multiplicity (report/conemap/slices), split_cuts, materialize_layers, record_vote_doubts, viewer/serve, slicevote itself (incl. its report page's img srcs). Experiment side - node_views, node_evidence, experiments/pool_retake, and judge_multiplicity's `rdir`.
- **CORRECTION TO THIS SESSION'S OWN CLAIM.** I said in conversation that no live stage consumed the experiment's renders. THAT IS WRONG: judge_multiplicity reads them for its panels AND validates its plan cameras against pool_targets.json. The experiment's output has a live judge downstream. What is true is narrower: node_views/node_evidence were the only NEW consumers, and node_views had no consumer until this session.
- **BUG I MADE AND CAUGHT:** a chained string replacement double-applied and produced `exp_exp_pool_retake/` in node_evidence's resolver - obj_042 then had "planned but no picture exists". Fixed; 48/48 attached again.
- **GOTCHA FOUND:** `node_views.py --only <ids>` REWRITES node_views.json with ONLY those nodes, so a partial run silently shrinks the file every downstream reader trusts. Worked around by re-running the full reshoot set; the file is not append-only and nothing says so.
- **VERIFIED:** all 9 touched modules py_compile; vote/{slicevote_report.json, conemap.json, slices, rows} and exp_pool_retake/pool_targets.json all resolve; node_evidence 48/48 attached with 0 problems; node_evidence page 0 broken image links; node_views page 128 links, 0 broken.
- **LOOK FOR:** the viewer's vote/slicevote layers still load; the next slicevote run writes into vote/ and not a recreated pool_retake/.
- **RENAMED, USER ORDER ("stop calling pool retake, its so unuseful"):** `exp_pool_retake/` -> **`aimed_views_resolved/`**, and the producer `experiments/pool_retake.py` -> **`experiments/render_aimed_views.py`** (git mv, history kept). The name now carries the fact that keeps biting - WHICH BOXES the pictures were aimed at - and pairs with graph/node_views/ (aimed views, CURRENT box). "Pool retake" named the experiment's internal jargon (a pool of candidates, a retake of earlier shots); neither fact matters to a reader.
- **THE SHAPE ON DISK NOW:** `vote/` (slicevote's own output) - `aimed_views_resolved/` (aimed views, resolved-era boxes, 506 files) - `graph/node_views/` (aimed views, current box) - `graph/node_evidence/` (the repairs + review page).
- **METHOD NOTE:** the rename used ONE exact non-overlapping token, not chained replacements - the `exp_exp_pool_retake` bug earlier this session came from a chain where the second substitution matched inside the first's output. Also fixed two stale output paths found while sweeping prose: `pool_retake/pool_report.json` and the viewer's `pool_retake/conemap.json` (conemap is slicevote's, so it now reads vote/).
- **VERIFIED AFTER BOTH RENAMES:** 0 code references to the old name remain (docs keep it as history); 7 modules py_compile; chain re-runs clean (48/48 attached, 40 re-cuts, graph/crops untouched at 246); node_evidence page 105 links 0 broken, node_views page 128 links 0 broken.
- **NAMING DRIFT FIXED (USER ORDER 2026-08-10: "please unify names, its very confusing if you keep calling it different things").** ONE NAME PER MODULE. The vote stage is `slicevote` - layer `voted`, folder `vote/`. Retired from code prose: "box-repair stage", "the vote-box stage", "the vote box stage" (build_voted, materialize_layers, scene_state, viewer/serve). ROOT CAUSE FIXED AT SOURCE: slicevote.py's own first line read "SLICE-VOTE VOTE - the box-repair stage", and reading a JOB DESCRIPTION as a second NAME is how the drift started; it now states the name first and carries the one-name rule in the docstring. Same discipline as the retired word "carve". Only the intentional teaching comment mentions the old phrasing.

## R-S2-77 - THE VOTE STAGE STOPS BORROWING: slicevote shoots its own plan view (USER ORDER, 2026-08-10)
- **USER RULING, Rule #1 restated in anger and correctly:** the goal is an unattended pipeline over 100 scenes, so "the aimed-views pass ran first, historically" has NO BEARING on any future scene. A stage may not depend on a batch some other method happened to leave behind.
- **WHAT WAS FOUND (the thread that led here).** J8 was not the real dependant - SLICEVOTE was. Its slice is a top-box vertical prism: detect the object's footprint in a PLAN VIEW, cast those corners down the height band. It never shot that plan view. It read aimed_views_resolved/<id>_{top,ctop}.png - a picture from experiments/render_aimed_views.py, the box method that LOST the 08-06 bake-off (R-S2-25 the promotion candidate, R-S2-26 the cone-map session that beat it). The winner was designed on the loser's renders because they were already on disk.
- **WHY NOBODY SAW IT:** both producers wrote into ONE folder called pool_retake/, so the read looked local. Splitting the folder this session (R-S2-76) is what exposed it.
- **AND THE SPLIT BROKE IT, briefly:** slicevote's `rdir` was doing double duty (its own output AND that foreign read); pointing rdir at vote/ left the plan-render read looking in the wrong place, where `if not png.exists(): continue` would have SILENTLY dropped the top voter and fallen back to the wedge. Caught before any run; nothing bad was produced.
- **THE FIX:** the plan render is now slicevote's own - `top_fit_render(nid, name, f"{nid}_{vname}", teye, c0, tfov, clip_ceiling=...)` in the top-camera loop. NO NEW MACHINERY: `vote_cams.top_cam_for` always RECOMPUTED those cameras (it never read them), top_fit_render is slicevote's renderer, and 18 of 55 plan views on living were ALREADY slicevote's because the borrowed camera could not frame the object. This makes the path that already ran a third of the time the only path.
- **J8 FOLLOWED:** its plan panel is captioned "the plan render the vote detected on", so it now reads vote/slices/. The legacy folder stays as a fallback ONLY for scenes whose vote predates this change, and it NAMES that in the notes rather than passing an old picture off as the current one.
- **WHAT A FRESH SCENE NEEDED BEFORE THIS:** run a demoted method's 206-render batch across 43 nodes to harvest 36 plan views and discard ~470. That is not a saving, it is a defect.
- **STATE:** slicevote no longer references aimed_views_resolved (one comment only). Remaining readers: judge_multiplicity (panels, fallback), node_views + node_evidence (the reuse gate, this session's work), and the producer itself.
- **NOT RUN. NEEDS A GO.** Re-running slicevote re-renders up to 55 plan views and can MOVE ELECTED BOXES: 37 nodes were detected on a resolved-era camera and would now be detected on a freshly aimed one. Different framing -> different detection -> possibly a different box. This is the stage that decides boxes, so it wants a run and a before/after comparison, not a rename.
- **LOOK FOR:** after the run, per-node box deltas vs the current voted layer; any node whose plan detection changes class (found/not found); the 18 topfit nodes should be UNCHANGED (they were already ours).

## R-S2-78 - THE SWEEP: no stage may depend on what a fresh scene will not produce (USER ORDER, 2026-08-10)
- **USER ORDER:** "do a sweep and clean up the pipeline before j9, so these dependencies like this isnt going to ruin us again." Rule #1 restated: 100 scenes, unattended, so anything that only exists because some one-off run happened is a DEFECT, not history.
- **THE RIGHT QUESTION, finally asked.** R-S2-72 asked "does this folder have a consumer?" - a test a demoted-but-load-bearing producer passes easily, which is why it reported clean. The question is "does every consumer read from a producer THE AUTOMATED CHAIN RUNS?"
- **METHOD:** scanned every non-experiment .py for scene-relative artifact names (280 distinct), then isolated the ones named by BOTH an experiment and a chain module, then traced each to where it actually lives on disk.
- **RESULT: only ONE cross-boundary producer existed** - experiments/render_aimed_views.py (aimed_views_resolved/ + pool_targets.json). Everything else that looked suspicious traced to a chain producer: lift_poolc.json and seg_batched20 are rig_sp0/ (the lift+seg stages), detections.json is seg_batched's, scene_manifest_pano2c_rc_f30.json is the manifest stage's, the compose/* artifacts are compose's own.
- **THE THREE CONSUMERS, ALL CUT:**
  1. slicevote read the plan render it detects on (R-S2-77) - now shoots its own.
  2. J8 read the plan panel + validated cameras against pool_targets.json - now reads vote/slices/ and the vote's OWN sidecar (votetgt_<id>_<view>.json). NO FALLBACK: a fallback fires only on THIS scene and hands the judge a stale picture under a caption saying otherwise.
  3. node_views' REUSE POOL was that folder - now its own prior renders, whose .params.json sidecars carry the same eye/aim/fov the old camera file did. node_evidence's second resolve path deleted for the same reason.
- **THE TELL THAT PROVES THE POINT:** with the reuse pool switched to our own store, living went from "0 still to render" to **26 still to render** - i.e. 26 of the 48 pictures the gate was about to write into the graph existed ONLY because a demoted method ran here once. Scenes 2..100 would have had none. Same code, two behaviours, and the unattended one never exercised.
- **A SECOND BUG FELL OUT:** the reuse key was `nid.split("#")[0]`, so a split piece (obj_011#1) could only ever match its PARENT's picture - a borrow dressed as a reuse. Now keyed by safe(nid), so obj_011#1 matches obj_011_p1 and nothing else.
- **VERIFIED:** zero EXECUTABLE references to aimed_views_resolved/pool_targets outside experiments/ (7 mentions remain, all comments recording why). All touched modules py_compile.
- **NOT RUN - THREE RUNS NOW OWED, IN ORDER:** (1) slicevote, which re-renders up to 55 plan views and CAN MOVE ELECTED BOXES (37 nodes move from a resolved-era camera to a freshly aimed one; the 18 that already used topfit are the control - if they move, the change is wrong); (2) node_views --render for the 26; (3) node_evidence, then J9. Each needs a go.

## R-S2-79 - THE OWED SLICEVOTE RUN, EXECUTED UNDER A GPU CLOCK LOCK (USER-PASSED, 2026-08-10)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: graph["resolved"] + the shell + the current code. DECIDES: the elected shipping box per node. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: a box that moved for a reason other than the code that was changed, or a run that reports success while serving cached pictures of a state that no longer exists.
- **THE MACHINE WAS THE BLOCKER, NOT THE CODE.** Four hard power-offs on 2026-08-10 (~16:20 bugcheck 0x19C, then ~18:39 / ~21:37 / ~21:57 with BugcheckCode 0 and no dump = power loss, not a fault). The 21:57 one killed a full vote run two minutes in. Full write-up: **docs/POWER_CRASHES.md**.
- **THE FIX THAT WORKED, RE-CONFIRMED:** `nvidia-smi -lgc 0,1500` (admin; `-pl` is OEM-locked on this laptop). Measured before/after on the same workload: peak clocks 2400 -> 1500 MHz, peak draw 139.99 -> 99.28 W, 0 of 843 samples above the ceiling. The run then completed in 13 minutes. NOTE the lock dies on reboot, so a crash always clears it and the retry after a crash runs unprotected - a boot-time scheduled task is written (tools/install_gpu_clock_lock.ps1) but NOT INSTALLED (needs one elevated click).
- **NEW TOOL:** tools/watch_gpu.ps1 - 1 Hz GPU+battery logger, one Add-Content per sample so the tail survives a power cut. Columns add ac_online/batt_rate_mW/batt_pct: if batt_rate_mW goes negative while on AC, the adapter is not covering the burst. It stayed 0 for all 843 samples of this run.
- **RESULT:** run_kind=full, mixed_provenance=False, **canon_eligible=True** (46/46: 27 voted_pano, 7 kept_wall, 7 kept_ceiling, 2 kept, 2 kept_outlier, 1 voted). The full re-run owed for canon since the poly-shell work is DONE.
- **DETERMINISM CHECKED, NOT ASSUMED.** obj_010 was voted twice under the same source_sha and params_hash (a one-object measurement run at 22:09, then inside the full run at 22:24). Every intermediate is identical to the digit - top pick 0.808/0.302, all four cards, top, sp0, plan_fill 1.6 | v2 0.867, VOTE 0.43 x 0.27 x 0.21. So box differences are attributable to code+params, not run-to-run drift. Caveat: one object, and it was an all-cache-hit path.
- **THE DIFF.** vs the pre-run state (44 of whose 46 boxes came from r20260808-203800 under the old sha/params): 11 boxes moved >= 0.02 m, 35 unchanged. 25 further rows differ ONLY by the retired `carved` -> `voted` word and are NOT changes. Biggest movers: obj_048 pillow (+242% vol), obj_038 window (-0.55 m), obj_012/obj_014 bookshelves (+0.37/+0.22 m deep).
- **NEW TOOLS FOR READING IT:** tools/vote_diff_sheet.py (per-object sheet with crops, per-axis deltas, and each row's BASE run id so "why did this move" is answerable on the page) and a temporary RED/GREEN A/B pair in viewer/serve.py box_sources so the moves can be SEEN on the splat rather than read off a table.
- **THE CONTROL WAS RAISED AND THE USER RULED ANYWAY - ON RECORD.** R-S2-77 pre-registered "the 18 topfit nodes should be UNCHANGED; if they move, the change is wrong". TWO topfit nodes moved: obj_048 and obj_016. This was put to the user before any pass was recorded, together with two complications: tonight's run shows 13 topfit nodes not 18 (so the control set is not the same set), and obj_048 re-framed AND re-shot (top -> topfit -> topfit2) so it is not cleanly "already ours" either. **USER RULING after looking at the red/green pair in the 3D viewer: PASS - "the differences are acceptable", and "a lot of the boxes will tighten in the next steps."** The tightening point is correct: the vote ships a measured box; narrowing it is compose/shopping's job.
- **NOT SETTLED, DO NOT LOSE:** only **6** plan views re-rendered fresh (obj_042_ctop, obj_046, obj_048, obj_063, obj_068, obj_069); **41** were served from cache, and some of those PNGs date from 08-06 / 08-08, i.e. BEFORE the R-S2-77 rewiring existed. That is either correct (vote_cams always recomputed the camera, so an unchanged camera legitimately hits cache) or it is the R-S2-67 / R-S2-76 failure class again - a stale artifact served silently under a new caption. NOT TRACED. R-S2-77 expected "up to 55" re-renders and got 6, so the rewiring is largely UNEXERCISED by this run.
- **CORRECTION TO A CLAIM MADE IN CONVERSATION:** I first attributed the 11 moved boxes to the poly-shell and rebox work. Wrong - the source_sha moved because of R-S2-77/78's plan-view rewiring, which is the likelier driver and is what makes obj_048 worth a second look. The determinism finding is unaffected.
- **LOOK FOR (next session):** whether the 41 cache hits are legitimate; whether obj_048's re-shoot path is doing the right thing; the amber materialized layer is still built on the OLD vote, so the graph wants a rebuild on this run before compose.
- **STILL TEMPORARY:** the vote_ab_old / vote_ab_new entries in viewer/serve.py box_sources. Remove once the user is done looking, same as the 08-06 prior pair.

## R-S2-80 - THE PLAN SHOT WAS INVISIBLE, AND ctop NEVER WORKED (USER-PASSED, 2026-08-10 late)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: the vote's plan ('top') shot per node. DECIDES: whether the slice is a measured PRISM or the fallback WEDGE. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: that decision being invisible to the reviewer, or a box shipping unmeasured while the run reports success.
- **HOW IT STARTED:** user reading cone_map.html - "the bookshelf includes the wall again, I thought we solved it". IT WAS NOT A REGRESSION. shell_clip is implemented and FIRED: obj_005/012/014 all stop 3.0-3.5 cm INSIDE the wall plane, none crosses. What the user actually saw was a DIFFERENT defect with the same look: obj_012/obj_014 are 2.80/2.83 m tall in a 3.06 m room - floor-to-ceiling. The clip removes volume OUTSIDE the shell; this is volume INSIDE it, which the clip was never meant to touch.
- **ROOT CAUSE, AND IT IS SYSTEMIC:** 11 of the 32 objects that get a plan shot FIND NOTHING and fall back to the original-box wedge. Slice paths this run: 20 PRISM, 11 FALLBACK WEDGE.
- **THE SPLIT IS ENTIRELY BY VIEW TYPE, NOT DISTANCE.** `top`: 22 detected / 1 failed. **`ctop`: 0 detected / 11 failed.** Every single ctop shot has failed. Distance is ruled out (ctop failed at 2.0 m, top succeeded at 5.8 m) and so are blank renders (measured: ctop images are 90% non-empty vs top's 99%). ctop is the FALLBACK camera - it goes above the ceiling, deletes the ceiling to see in, and tilts only 10 deg (vs top's 15), so it is handed the hardest objects BY DESIGN: the tall ones and the ones high on shelves. All 3 bookshelves, all 5 magazines, the floor lamp, plant and tv stand.
- **THE OLD BORROWED SHOTS WERE THE SAME PICTURES.** aimed_views_resolved/ still holds the pre-R-S2-77 renders. Compared pixel-wise: mean |diff| 0.02-9.1 / 255, most under 2. Same camera, same view, same ctop choice. So (a) R-S2-77's claim that only the FILE was borrowed is confirmed, (b) the detection failure is OLD, not caused by the rewiring, and (c) those cache hits were LEGITIMATE - which closes part of R-S2-79's open "were the 41 cache hits stale?" question.
- **WHAT THE FALLBACK ACTUALLY DOES (why it never corrects anything):** the wedge keeps every point whose LEFT-RIGHT screen position falls in the original 2D box, then cuts above the ceiling. No top/bottom limit, no depth limit - a floor-to-ceiling slab of room. obj_012: 169,567 candidate dots, 95,327 of them shell-ineligible, and card0 claimed 167,705 = 99% of the slab. The fallback does not measure badly; IT MEASURES NOTHING NEW, and the object keeps the shape it arrived with while the run reports a clean result.
- **THE HEIGHT IS NOT A VOTE BUG - IT CAME IN THAT WAY.** obj_012's candidates: original 2.705, strict 2.756, vote2 2.808, pano 2.800. Every method agrees. Whatever set that height is UPSTREAM of the vote. STILL UNEXPLAINED, do not let it drop.
- **FIXED AT SOURCE - the page now shows the step that decides.** The row builder skipped view "top" by design, which hid the DETECTION shot (the "TOP VOTER" figure is a different thing - the slice seen from above). Now every rung of the re-shoot ladder is drawn with its overlay, a shot that found nothing is CAPTIONED as a failure rather than silently absent, and the stats line carries "PLAN SHOT FOUND NOTHING - this box was NOT re-measured". Regenerated by re-running the real writer, NOT by a second tool writing into vote/rows - a pages-only mode would be another reconstruction, and this log already records two cases of the page flattering itself.
- **SHELL_EPS 0.03 -> 0.05, USER RULING ("3cm was arbitrary anyway, try 5").** Evidence: obj_012's vote box ran 0.74 m further along the wall than pano; the 11,684 dots it gained sit at a MEDIAN 4.2 cm from the wall plane, 84% in the 2-5 cm band - just outside a 3 cm cut. Over all four walls 3 cm captures only 29% of wall-adjacent material, and wall_00's DENSEST band is 3-4 cm, so the old cut landed on the RISING EDGE of the wall's own material. ⚠ Labelled in the code as chosen after seeing one scene's symptom. TWO EXPLANATIONS STILL FIT AND ARE NOT SEPARATED: a genuinely ~5 cm fuzzy wall, OR a plane fitted 3-4 cm outside the true surface. Deciding test: plane-fit residuals across several rooms.
- **RESULT: 14 boxes changed, ALL SHRANK, none grew.** Walls improved: obj_012 -0.203 m (vol -20.5%), obj_014 -0.081, obj_032/036 magazines -18/-25% vol, obj_042 tv stand -0.105. CONTROL: obj_022 (plant, on a shelf 1.77 m up) tightened in x/z with its bottom UNMOVED - the wall effect isolated from the floor effect.
- **⚠ SIDE EFFECT, FOUND BY MEASURING NOT BY EYE: one constant governs walls, floor AND ceiling.** Every floor-standing object lifted OFF the floor: obj_028 chair's bottom gap 0.121 -> 0.267 m (lost 0.146 m of height, 19.6% of volume), obj_020 0.155 -> 0.199, obj_041 0.113 -> 0.150, obj_006 coffee table 0.066 -> 0.103, both sofas -0.024. Note obj_028 lost 14.6 cm from a 2 cm threshold move - removing the bottom voters changes WHICH CLUSTER WINS, so the effect is not proportional.
- **USER PASS** on the red/green A/B in the 3D viewer (SHELL_EPS 0.03 vs 0.05, everything else identical - same renders, same cameras, only who may VOTE changed).
- **OPEN, NEEDS A RULING:** split the constant (5 cm at walls, floor/ceiling back to 3 cm) vs keep 5 cm everywhere. EVERYTHING MEASURED WAS MEASURED AT WALLS and says nothing about the floor's splat thickness, so the floor was changed without evidence.
- **NOT DONE:** graph rebuild on this run (voted was built from r20260810-222445, the manifest is now r20260811-000149); then split_cuts + materialize, which is what brings J8's rulings - including obj_018's small box - back into settled/grouped. obj_018 is CORRECT as it stands: big box in `voted` (it is kept_ceiling, the vote never applies J8), small box still in settled 0.165x0.051x0.158 and grouped 0.199x0.031x0.140.
- **STILL TEMPORARY:** vote_ab_old / vote_ab_new in viewer/serve.py, now repointed to the 0.03-vs-0.05 pair.
- **RULING ON THE OPEN SPLIT (user, 2026-08-10 late): KEEP 5 cm EVERYWHERE, do not split the constant.** Reasoning given: there is margin to play with on big objects, and the downstream snapping stage will likely fix the floor problem. VERIFIED, and it holds: compose/snap.py places each object on its supporter with TOP_TOL = 0.35 m, and obj_028's 0.267 m gap sits inside that window, so the floating boxes will be put back on the floor. ⚠ ONE CONSEQUENCE ON RECORD: snap SHIFTS a box (mn and mx move together), it does not GROW it - so a floor-standing object keeps the height the wider filter cost it (obj_028: -0.146 m) and its TOP lands that much low, which is what shopping will be asked to match. Acceptable if the fitter's slack absorbs it; revisit here if picks come back short.

## R-S2-81 - THE EVIDENCE BECAME A LAYER, AND THE PICTURES WERE ALREADY ON DISK (2026-08-11)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: settled boxes + every picture the scene owns. DECIDES: the ONE picture each node is seen as. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: a judge handed a picture of the wrong region, or a repair planned for a node already served.
- **THE PAGE WAS LYING IN THREE PLACES, ALL THE SAME BUG.** A reshoot node with no aimed picture yet fell through to a RE-CUT PREVIEW - advertising the repair it was denied (R-S2-75 fixed this only for the case where a picture exists; run without --reshoot and it came straight back). A `keep` node showed a preview of a re-cut it will never get. And the table listed ONLY nodes that fire, collapsing the rest into one sentence, so the decisions NOT to repair - half of what there is to judge - could not be seen. All three fixed: every node listed, and each row shows the file a judge will actually be handed or says plainly that there is none.
- **JARGON OUT (user: "the tags are stupid, tell me the actual problem").** escaped/not_in_photo/borrowed/rezoomed are internal keys and stay in the JSON and CSS; the PAGE now says "the tile cuts the object off", "the object runs off the edge of the photo", "this is the parent's photo, not its own", "the tile is at the wrong zoom". Column "the photo" -> "the whole photo it was cut from".
- **THE EVIDENCE IS NOW VISUAL (user: "i want to see the direct evidence visualized").** Drawing the box ON the photo could never show "runs off the edge" - the proof is the part outside the frame, which was clipped away with the drawing. The photo is now pasted on a larger canvas with the box drawn at FULL extent crossing the edge, a white outline marking the photograph's own boundary, and the deciding numbers printed beside it (in_photo / in_crop / zoom, each with its threshold and verdict). 12 of 44 canvases needed padding, i.e. the box genuinely overflows.
- **THE BIG ONE: 33 OF 35 REPAIRS WERE UNNECESSARY.** User asked why the vote's own renders were not being reused. They were not in the pool - node_evidence only looked at detector crops, while ~217 aimed renders with recorded cameras sat in vote/slices. Wired in and measured by the SAME rule: **repair plan went 24 recut + 11 reshoot -> 1 recut + 1 reshoot.** Legitimate under R-S2-78 (the vote is a CHAIN stage that runs on every scene, not a demoted experiment). USER RULING on the caveat: the renders are cone-culled so occluders are deleted - "culling is fine, use them anyway" - and the page marks those 7 tiles "Occluders removed" rather than passing them off as photographs.
- **AND THE TWO SURVIVORS ARE EXACTLY WHAT THE MODULE IS FOR** (user: "i only added this module for things that moved significantly and for things that are just invented"): obj_018 (J8 swapped its big box for the small one, so the stored rectangle no longer contains it) and obj_011#1 (a split piece whose crops belong to its parent). Nothing else needed anything.
- **CAMERA RULE LOOSENED (user).** Eye level always, and pull a camera back toward the CAPTURE STANDPOINT instead of dropping the view. Measured justification: of 34 culled cameras, 21% were outside by <= 0.30 m and 56% by <= 1.00 m, and the keep-out is ITSELF 0.30 m - obj_014's perpB was thrown away over ONE CENTIMETRE. Culls 114 -> 53, views 220 -> 281, obj_011#1 3 -> 8. **I GOT IT WRONG FIRST TIME** and pulled toward the OBJECT; that fails for exactly the nodes needing help, because a wall-hugging object sits INSIDE the keep-out so every point near it is illegal. Toward the standpoint always has somewhere to arrive. New drop reason `prune_redundant` (a pulled camera landing within 0.35 m of another is the same picture twice) - an honest cull, unlike the old one. Every kept view records `pulled_in_m`.
- **`shown` IS NOW A LAYER (USER RULING: "these tiles supersede the old ones, its not just to judge, its an evolution for the scene state").** graph['shown'] = settled's nodes and edges VERBATIM plus a `shown` block per node naming its current picture. 45/45 nodes carry one, 0 problems, every path resolves. Sources: 33 vote renders, 10 stored crops, 1 re-cut, 1 aimed render. Sits between `settled` and `grouped` because node_evidence reads settled BY NAME - grouped is J9's own output.
- **J9 READS IT (B5, the consumer that never existed).** judge_same_product picked crops by DETECTION SCORE - the exact evidence this module flags as untrustworthy - so 35 of 45 nodes were being matched on pictures of the wrong region. Now reads graph['shown'], the shown picture WINS OUTRIGHT over the stored crops it supersedes, and the run prints which it used. Degrades to the old pick (and says so) when the layer is absent or stale, so a scene that skipped node_evidence still completes.
- **STALE LAYERS NOW RESOLVE THEMSELVES, FOR THE 100-SCENE GOAL.** Writing `shown` into an already-built chain left `grouped` newer-by-order but built from older inputs, and scene_state.check() reported a DISAGREEMENT a human had to interpret - fine on one scene, a stall on a hundred. `stamp()` now marks every layer AFTER the one written stale (marked, never deleted, with the reason), present()/current() skip stale layers, and a layer stops being stale when its own stage rewrites it. On a fresh scene the sweep does nothing.
- **AUTOMATION SCAN, USER-REQUESTED -> docs/AUTOMATION_READINESS.md.** THE CHAIN IS NOT AUTOMATABLE TODAY. Three blockers: (1) SIX of ten stages do NOTHING without a flag and exit 0 doing it - record_vote_doubts/build_voted/rederive/materialize need --apply, node_views needs --render, node_evidence needs three; a runner that forgets one gets success and no work; (2) node_evidence writes a layer with HOLES and calls it done (43/45 with `problems: 2`, exit 0); (3) nothing gates on a run FINISHING - a scene can end with grouped stale and report success. Plus run_scene.py stops at lift_pano and knows none of steps 1-11. The doc carries the verified command order, the per-scene scan, the surviving defects (ctop 0/11, --only rewriting node_views.json, the un-installed GPU clock task) and the order to fix them in.
- **ORDER GOTCHA FOUND WHILE WRITING IT UP:** materialize_layers writes `settled` with --settle-only and `grouped` without. Running the FULL one at step 7 writes grouped from a state J9 has not seen - which is what happened on 08-11 and why grouped ended the night stale. Geometry first, group last.
- **NOT DONE:** the four fixes above; J9 has only been dry-run (grounding page built, no LLM calls); grouped is stale pending a real J9 + materialize.

## R-S2-82 - THE MAIN PHOTO: SEARCHED POSITION, SOLVED LENS (2026-08-11, user-passed)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: a node's settled box, the room shell, the splat. DECIDES: the ONE camera the main photo is taken from. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: an object that does not fit in its own main photo, or a camera far from where the room was actually seen.
- **THE RULE (user).** Main photo comes from the DEFAULT VIEWPOINT, in this order: the stored crop if it still frames the box -> a re-cut of that same photograph -> and only then a new render. The new render is at EYE LEVEL, at the position NEAREST THE CAPTURE STANDPOINT that can frame and see the object. Explicitly NOT "widen the lens": the user wanted the defence against truly large objects to be the room's geometry, not a fisheye.
- **I BROKE THE LADDER FIRST AND HAD TO PUT IT BACK.** For about an hour a vote render was promoted to MAIN whenever one framed the box, which took the plan from 24 re-cuts to 1 - not because re-cutting stopped being right but because it had been demoted below a cone-culled picture of whatever side the vote's camera happened to see. USER CAUGHT IT: "we can still be cropping it no? we saw these objects in `the crop it shows now`". Restored: 24 recut / 11 reshoot / 10 keep, and the vote renders ride along as SUPPLEMENTARY views (235 of them, median 5 per node).
- **WHY A SEARCH BEATS FOUR COMPASS POINTS.** The room is 4.7 m wide and 8.4 m long. An object that cannot be framed from the side is often framable along the length; fixing the direction to card0..3 and dropping when that direction failed threw those solutions away. The search walks a 0.25 m floor grid at eye level, sorted by distance from the standpoint, and takes the FIRST position that is in the room, not inside furniture, has a clear line of sight, and can frame the box at <= 75 deg. Result: 45/45 nodes get a main view, median 0.68 m from the standpoint, 9 of 11 reshoots within 1.13 m. obj_042 (3.4 m tv stand) had to walk 2.26 m and tried 235 standpoints.
- **THREE FRAMING BUGS, ALL MINE, ALL FOUND BY MEASURING.**
  1. Far bound set by FOV_MIN=35 (a natural-lens floor meant for the cardinal views) demanded the camera stand 0.55 m from a ceiling light -> **18 of 45 nodes got NO main view**. Zooming IN is free on a render; only the wide end distorts. MAIN_FOV_MIN=10.
  2. Radius taken as `max(size)/2` - the sphere that fits INSIDE the box, not one that CONTAINS it. **8 of 11 main views clipped** (obj_016 at 61%). Fixed to the half-diagonal: 8 -> 3.
  3. Lens then sized with `atan(r/d)`, a sphere's tangent, when a sphere subtends `asin(r/d)` and a BOX's corners project further still. 3 -> 0.
- **THE FIX THAT HOLDS IS STRUCTURAL, NOT ARITHMETIC.** The lens is now MEASURED: project the eight real corners, take the largest |x|/z and |y|/z in camera space, `fov = 2 atan(ratio * margin)`. A standpoint needing more than FOV_MAX is REJECTED and the search moves on - so "the main photo contains the whole object" is true by construction. VERIFIED by projecting every box into its own camera: **11/11 at 100% in frame**, fill 38-86%, lenses 21-73 deg, none at the ceiling.
- **CLOSED FORM, NOT ITERATION (user: "cant we use deterministic ways?").** The first version rendered with a trial fov, measured the pixel overshoot and rescaled. It converged on pass one because the overshoot is LINEAR in tan(fov/2) - the loop's second pass only confirmed. Replaced with the direct solve; identical positions and lenses, no re-render. The camera BASIS never depended on fov (make_cam builds it from eye/aim/up), only the focal scale did.
- **WHAT IS STILL A SEARCH, AND WHY IT MUST BE.** Position cannot be closed-form: two constraints are queries against 1M gaussians (is the eye inside furniture; is the sight line blocked). But the search is DETERMINISTIC - fixed grid, fixed order, first pass wins - which matters because render fingerprints key on the camera, so an unchanged scene re-runs to identical pictures and skips the GPU.
- **KNOWN, NOT FIXED:** the older `standoff()` used by the cardinal views has the SAME `max(size)/2` underestimate. FILL=1.5 gives it more slack so it bites less, but those views will be clipping some objects. Untouched deliberately - changing it invalidates every existing cardinal render.
- **LEFT OPEN:** the 0.25 m grid step is the one number nothing measured justifies (a trade between finding a good position and the per-candidate occlusion cost).
- **METHOD NOTE, worth carrying:** I reported step 2 as working and would have gone straight to J9 with 8 of 11 main photos clipped - the code looked right and the log looked clean. Nothing being printed would have caught it. What caught it was the user looking at the pictures, and what fixed it was printing a NUMBER PER NODE (in-frame %) so each round could be checked instead of believed.
- **INCIDENT:** corrupted graph/view_cams.py mid-session with a PowerShell Get-Content/Set-Content round-trip (PS 5.1 writes a BOM and had already misread UTF-8 as ANSI - every em-dash mangled). Repaired by reversing the mojibake and stripping the BOM; verified no other file affected. USE Edit FOR FILE CHANGES, never a shell rewrite.
- **TWO MORE, BOTH USER-FOUND BY LOOKING AT THE PICTURES (2026-08-11, later).**
