  1. **THE CAMERA WAS 0.66 m TOO LOW ON EVERY NODE.** "Eye level" was coded as `floor - EYE_H` (a 1.6 m standing person); the actual capture standpoint sits 2.26 m above this floor. So all 45 main photos were taken two-thirds of a metre below the viewpoint the room was seen from — a constant offset, invisible in every number being printed. Now `stand_y = eye0[1]`, with a fallback only if the standpoint is outside the shell. AND the standpoint itself is now CANDIDATE ZERO: a 0.25 m grid never lands on it, so "just turn the camera you already have" was never actually offered. Result: **7 of 11 main views are now 0.00 m from the standpoint** — the default camera, turned. The rest move 0.17 / 0.55 / 0.66 / 1.48 m (obj_042, the 3.4 m tv stand, after 113 standpoints).
  2. **`card0` WAS MASQUERADING AS THE MAIN PHOTO ON 9 OF 11 NODES.** chosen_picture took `reshoot_files[0]`. When the `main` render was missing it silently promoted the first cardinal instead — a view from wherever the standoff rule put the camera, shipped as the object's main photo. It logged a problem and shipped anyway. Cause of the missing files was mine too: the closed-form fov gave float-level different values, which correctly deleted 9 stale pngs, and I ran that pass WITHOUT --render so they were never redrawn. FIXED AT SOURCE: the main view is now selected BY NAME and its absence is a PROBLEM, never a substitution — the cardinals are already carried as supplementary and do not need promoting. Attach 75 -> 84 pictures, 0 problems.
- **THE METHOD LESSON, THIRD TIME TONIGHT:** every number being printed (in-frame %, distance-to-standpoint, attach counts) was TRUE and none of them could catch either bug — one was a constant offset, the other a file coming from the wrong camera under the right name. Three rounds of "I reported it working, the user looked at the pictures and it wasn't". For an unattended run this is the gap: a metric that is true per-node still cannot tell you the picture is of the wrong thing.

## R-S2-83 - CLOSING THE SESSION: the clock lock is automatic, and a correction (2026-08-11)
- **INSTALLED.** Scheduled task `GPUClockLock` — `nvidia-smi -lgc 0,1500` as SYSTEM at every system startup, on-battery restrictions explicitly disabled. R-S2-79 says it was written but NOT INSTALLED; that was true then and is superseded here. The crash-clears-the-lock hole is CLOSED: after a crash the lock is back before anyone logs in, which matters because the failure IS a reboot.
- **A CLAIM I MADE WAS WRONG, ON RECORD.** I said that once the task existed an unelevated session could fire it with `schtasks /run /tn GPUClockLock`, and used that as an argument for the task over a plain UAC prompt. It is FALSE: a task running as SYSTEM is neither visible nor startable by a standard user — access denied on query, on run, and on reading the task file. What the task actually buys is the BOOT case. Re-applying mid-session still needs an admin shell.
- **PS 5.1 READS `.ps1` AS ANSI UNLESS THERE IS A BOM.** install_gpu_clock_lock.ps1 would not run at all: four em-dashes became mojibake, one landed inside a quoted string, and the parser reported a missing closing brace twenty lines away. watch_gpu.ps1 had the SAME fault and survived only because its em-dashes sat in comments. Both forced to plain ASCII (a8f369d). Any non-ASCII character in a .ps1 is a latent parse bomb on this shell.
- **AND THE RELATED ONE:** graph/view_cams.py was corrupted mid-session by a PowerShell Get-Content/Set-Content round-trip — PS 5.1's Get-Content misread the UTF-8 as ANSI and Set-Content -Encoding utf8 wrote it back with a BOM, mangling every em-dash. Repaired by reversing the mojibake and stripping the BOM; no other file affected. NEVER round-trip a source file through the shell; use the editor.
- **COMMITTED, WORKING TREE CLEAN:** dcee7e7 (the evidence layer + the machine), a8f369d (the ASCII fix), 54600d1 (these doc corrections). Seven sessions of work were uncommitted before this.
- **STATE AT CLOSE:** scene_state.check() True, current layer `shown` (45/45 nodes with a picture, 0 problems), `grouped` correctly stale pending J9. Vote canon_eligible=True on r20260811-000149. **J9 IS READY AND UNRUN — sheets built, no model calls spent.** Read SESSION_2026-08-24_HANDOFF.md.

## R-S2-84 - THE CHAIN RUNS ITSELF (2026-08-11 overnight, orchestrated, PROVISIONAL - unreviewed)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: a scene whose record/judged/resolved layers exist. DECIDES: nothing about the scene - this work decides only WHETHER THE MACHINE RAN. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: a scene reported as finished that is not, or a stage that did nothing and was believed.
- **THE ASK (user, going to sleep):** "it cannot run autonomously from top to bottom... make sure we can run 100 scenes automated with smooth and no problems, with clear boundaries between modules, and clear state checkpoints inbetween."
- **THE ORDER IS NOW DATA, NOT PROSE.** `graph/stages.py` is one row per stage: the command, the layer it READS, the layer it WRITES, the files it must produce, whether it costs model calls or GPU. The eleven commands appear in no runner and in no document any more. `run_scene.py --list` prints the table. Adding a stage means adding a row.
- **THE CHECKPOINT IS `graph/scene_gate.py`, AND IT ASKS WHAT NO STAGE CAN ASK ABOUT ITSELF.** `before`: is the layer this stage reads present and NOT STALE. `after`: did this stage really write, JUST NOW, what it promised - and is that layer now the state of the scene. `final`: did the run FINISH. Exit 3 for a gate failure, distinct from 1 (crash) and 2 (node_evidence refusing an evidence layer with holes).
- **THE MTIME TEST IS THE WHOLE POINT.** `after` requires each promised file to have been written DURING the stage's own run. Existence proves only that some earlier run made it. Demonstrated on the clone: told that `doubts` had just run when it had not, the gate answered *"exists but was NOT written by this run (last written 10097s before the stage started)"*. That is the silent no-op - the hazard this whole exercise is about - caught mechanically.
- **THE SIX NO-OP FLAGS ARE INVERTED.** Writing is the DEFAULT in record_vote_doubts, build_voted, rederive_voted_edges, materialize_layers and node_evidence; `--dry-run` is the opt-out; node_views uses `--no-render`. `--apply` / `--render` / `--recut` / `--reshoot` still parse and DO NOTHING - stated loudly in the docs, because an old command line in a handoff now misleads in the opposite direction. Proved on the clone: `--dry-run` left the graph byte-identical, the plain run wrote it and swept `settled, shown, grouped` stale.
- **node_evidence REFUSES TO WRITE A LAYER WITH HOLES**, exit 2, naming the nodes; `--allow-holes` overrides and records the gap IN the layer. A gap in the evidence stops the scene, because a judge downstream reads that layer as proof. Also made `--dry-run` genuinely inert - it was still wiping and rebuilding the recut directory.
- **PROVEN END TO END.** On `autotest_living` (a 2.0 GB clone, made so the live scene was never at risk): ten of eleven stages - everything but the hour-long vote - driven by ONE command through the new `run_fleet.py`, no human in the loop, every gate green before and after every stage, 1m59s, final gate PASS on `grouped` with no stale layers and 46/46 nodes carrying a picture. **This is the first run of this pipeline whose completion was checked rather than assumed.** `living_marble` was not touched: its scene_graph.json is untouched since 02:49 and its J9 gate is still the user's to decide.
- **`run_fleet.py` FOR THE HUNDRED.** Scene list / file / discovery, `--exclude`, `--resume` (skips scenes whose gate already passes), `--scene-timeout` (default 4 h), `--stop-on-fail`. One bad scene never stops the night - verified with a deliberately broken scene, which failed at its first gate in 0.0 s while the good scene ran and passed, fleet exit 1. The morning table carries the gate's WARN/INFO lines beside the verdict, because a scene can PASS and still be poor.
- **A SECOND AUDIT FOUND WHAT THE READINESS DOC MISSED, AND MOST OF IT IS FIXED.** `scene_graph.json` - 1.5 MB, the whole scene, NOT re-derivable - was rewritten non-atomically by four stages, two of them right after a GPU stage on a machine whose failure mode is a power cut; now `paths.write_atomic` everywhere. A corrupt preview manifest was swallowed and the file rewritten with THIS RUN'S ids only (every other object's box gone, exit 0); now fatal. The `.ply` header parser looped forever on a truncated file, appending `b""` until the machine swapped to death; now bounded. A truncated `_box.png` was kept forever because staleness there was mtime-only, and handed to J9 as the node's one picture; now verified and re-made.
- **THE WORST ONE: A MODEL OUTAGE WAS BECOMING A SCENE FULL OF CONFIDENT DEFAULTS.** Auth, invalid-key and credit-balance errors were caught by the same handler as a timeout, so an expired token at scene 40 would give every J8 case `UNCLEAR conf 0.00`, ship every J8s region uncut, leave every J9 member unassigned - and every gate would pass on all 60 remaining scenes. Those three are now fatal. **And the check was in the wrong order:** a 401 also exits non-zero, so the old code raised a plain exit-code error and never reached the credential test - the fix would not have fired on the most likely real failure. Every stage now also records `judge_failures {defaulted, total, ids}`; NO threshold was invented, because where that line falls is the user's call.
- **FOUND BY ACTUALLY RE-RUNNING THE CHAIN: THERE IS NO JUDGE FOR A DUPLICATE THE VOTE ITSELF CREATES.** The clone came out with 46 settled nodes against living's 45. J1 answers SAME_CANDIDATE edges on the RECORD, before the vote; the vote then moves every box and can propose a candidate no judge ever sees - here two chairs, obj_020 and obj_068, 96% contained in one another. materialize merges only SAME verdicts, so an unjudged candidate silently ships as two objects. living_marble HAS that merge, recorded 08-10, but its `voted` was rebuilt 08-11 and the rebuild wiped the verdict, so the merge survives only as a fossil in a layer whose inputs are gone. **NOT fixed - it is a design decision.** The gate now WARNs on it, on every scene.
- **NOT DONE, DELIBERATELY:** `ctop` 0-for-11 (a design question about how the vote sees tall and flat objects, and the chain runs without it); J8's concurrency of 8, which multiplies GPU renders and not just model lanes, because reversing a user ruling is not a repair; WSL renders surviving a Python timeout; `shown` storing supplementary view paths absolute. All recorded in docs/PLAN_AUTOMATION_2026-08-11.md.
- **HONEST LIMIT: ONE SCENE IS NOT A HUNDRED.** What is proved is that the chain completes unattended and that the gate catches a no-op, a stale input and a broken scene. The 100-scene claim is readiness, not evidence.

## R-S2-85 - THE BOUNDARY AUDIT, AND THE BLOCKER ONLY A FRESH SCENE COULD SHOW (2026-08-11, PROVISIONAL - unreviewed)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: the chain as built in R-S2-84. DECIDES: nothing about any scene - this pass only asks whether the MACHINE's promises are true. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: a declared boundary that is not what the code does, so the gate waves through the very thing it was built to catch.
- **THE DECLARATIONS WERE ONLY HALF TRUE.** 4 of 11 stage rows were accurate; 6 declared a layer they never read. Every layer-level error leaned SAFE (a stage declares a layer at or later than the one it truly reads, and the stale sweep makes "N is fresh" imply "nothing before N moved"). The dangerous errors were all at the FILE level, where no staleness mechanism existed at all.
- **HOLE A - THE STALENESS MACHINERY ONLY UNDERSTOOD LAYERS.** graph[vote], graph[voted_edges] and the three judge sidecars are inputs to settled that could neither be stale nor make anything stale. Re-run j8 alone, it rewrites multiplicity.json, settled was built from it, and every layer still reports fresh with check() passing. FIXED: scene_state.stamp records written_at; stages.py declares each stage's file inputs; scene_gate.stale_inputs compares them. Proven both directions on a live scene.
- **HOLE B - FIVE MODULES WROTE A LAYER AND NEVER STAMPED IT.** materialize_verdicts (resolved), build_judged (judged), build_edges (record), migrate_walls_w5 (three at once), compose/support_clip --apply (resolved, in place). One flag silently invalidated a finished scene's whole downstream stack while both freshness answers still agreed. FIXED. The gate then found this in the wild, unprompted, on the fresh scene.
- **HOLE C - THE WRONG BLOCK WAS READ FOR J1 MERGES.** same_verdict_pairs' docstring named voted_edges over code that preferred voted. So re-judging a duplicate pair after the vote DID NOTHING - exactly the repair R-S2-84's two-chairs problem calls for. FIXED: both blocks read, voted_edges winning.
- **--only WAS DELETING WORK IT WAS NOT ASKED ABOUT.** split_cuts.json was rewritten with ONLY the named cases, so materialize shipped the rest UNCUT while J8 still said to split them - silently, gate passing. node_views.json had the same shape. Both now merge-on-write, both refuse a corrupt file rather than starting over.
- **A SCENE WITH NO SPLITS WAS A HARD FAILURE.** split_cuts exited non-zero on an empty J8 docket - an ordinary, correct outcome. Would have failed most of the hundred.
- **ISOLATION: FILES CLEAN, GPU NOT.** Verified rather than assumed - every stage writes only inside its scene folder, every temp file carries a node id, every cache is per-scene, no glob can reach another scene, no CWD dependency, the bedroom vs bedroom_marble prefix hazard does not exist. The GPU had no lock anywhere in the repo, and J8 alone already put 8 renders on the card at once. Added paths.gpu_lock (all five WSL render sites) and paths.scene_lock. Stale locks clear on PID LIVENESS, not a timeout - the failure being survived is a power cut. os.kill(pid, 0) is NOT the liveness test on Windows: it calls TerminateProcess and would kill the process it asks about.
- **PROVED BY AN ACCIDENT.** A mistyped --until settled (no --from, so it starts at vote) began a 40-minute vote on the finished clone and was killed hard mid-render. Both lock files outlived their owners; the next acquirer broke each in 0.01s and 0.00s naming the dead pid, and the scene itself was undamaged.
- **A PARTIAL RUN IS NOW INCOMPLETE, NOT FAIL.** The first long run finished --until vote successfully and was then reported FAIL, because the final gate asked whether the chain reached grouped. It had not, because that is what was asked for. Reporting an obeyed instruction as a failure is how a gate teaches people to ignore it.
- **THE BLOCKER ONLY A FRESH SCENE COULD SHOW.** materialize_layers --settle-only - the GEOMETRY pass that runs BEFORE J9 - required J9's same_product.json in its constructor. On a fresh scene that file cannot exist, so --settle-only could never succeed on a scene that had not already been through the chain once. Invisible because every dev scene had a stale copy from an earlier session. A hundred-scene run would have failed on scene 1. FIXED: optional in the constructor, required in run() where the verdicts are applied. LESSON: test on a scene that has never run.
- **THE THREE-SCENE RUN.** Deliberately unlike each other: a fresh 82-node bedroom, a finished living room, and a mid-chain clone named as a prefix-sibling of the first. First pass 2/3 - the bedroom hit the blocker above and the other two ran and passed while it was failing, which is the isolation property the fleet exists for. After the fix the bedroom passed too: 82/82 nodes with a picture, chain on grouped, nothing stale. 3 of 3.
- **WHERE THE TIME GOES** (user asked for per-module timing): views 59%, j8 22%, j9 15%, j8s 3%; the seven bookkeeping stages together under two seconds. On the fresh scene views alone was 81%.
- **A QUALITY FINDING THE TABLE SURFACED: 55 of 82 objects (67%) on the fresh bedroom were never re-measured** - the vote fell back to a full-height wedge - against 9 of 46 (20%) on living. Same machinery, three times worse on a room it was not developed against. ctop remains out of scope by ruling, but it is far more expensive than one scene suggested.
- **THE MACHINE:** 17,139 GPU samples on 08-11, peak 1500 MHz, peak 104 W, ZERO above the lock, including under J8's 8 workers. No crashes. (Unlocked July record on the same card: 2415 MHz, 203 W.)
- **NOT FIXED, USER'S CALL:** every module in compose/ reads boxes from resolved, the PRE-VOTE layer, so placement and shopping run against geometry the vote replaced - the biggest correctness finding of the night, and outside the chain so nothing built here catches it. And materialize's full pass rebuilds grouped from voted rather than inheriting from shown, so the per-node picture the evidence layer exists to decide does not survive into the final layer.

## R-S2-86 - THE GLTS BASELINE, RUN AND SCORED (2026-08-11, PROVISIONAL - unreviewed)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: the Marble prompt a scene was generated from - the one input both methods genuinely share. DECIDES: nothing about either method's quality; it measures four axes and labels each COMPARABLE or ASYMMETRIC. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: a number that quietly credits one method for something it did not do, or a denominator that measures nothing.
- **glts_run.py.** Isolation is GLTS's own PROJECT_ROOT, one directory per scene. Blender phase takes paths.gpu_lock, layout phase does not - which is what makes --parallel worth having: the slow model-bound part overlaps, the part that can power off the machine does not. Verified: both scenes ran side by side through 13 stages, GPU idle at ~36 W throughout, 53.7 min wall clock against 60.2 sequential.
- **GLTS EXITS 0 WHEN THE SCENE FAILED.** The bedroom reported success in 6.5 min having stopped at step 11: the model returned 26 characters for a field GLTS caps at 25 ("white duvet, green blanket"), retried 3 times, gave up, wrote failed_instructions.json - and returned 0, because the launcher is a shell script whose last command succeeded. THE SAME HAZARD THIS PIPELINE WAS AUDITED FOR ALL SESSION, and I walked into it on the baseline. Completion is now judged by the artifact the last step writes plus GLTS's own failure log.
- **THE FAILURE WAS OURS, NOT THEIRS.** The 25-char cap is GLTS's schema; the overshoot is Claude's, and GPT-4o (the paper's backend) complied. Reporting it as a GLTS failure would blame the baseline for our model substitution. Fixed in OUR bridge (utils/get_claude_agent.py): the retry now states the length rule explicitly instead of only echoing the pydantic error. Bedroom then completed - 77.2 min, 164 calls.
- **TWO NUMBERS WERE MISLEADING AND WERE FIXED BY SAYING MORE, NOT BY MAKING EITHER METHOD LOOK BETTER.**
  1. **The prompt-fidelity denominator measured nothing.** vocab_from_prompt builds a DETECTION vocabulary, where a spare word is free, so it returned sense, elegance, comfort, warmth, texture, illumination. Neither method can place an elegance. The living room read "23 nouns, ours named 6" when ours had named SIX OF THE SEVEN placeable things asked for. Filtered by a published stop-list (kept and dropped lists both in the report). Bedroom's 18 nouns needed no filtering - that prompt is genuinely concrete.
  2. **Floating slandered the reconstruction.** GLTS scores 0 because it PLACES objects on a floor it invented - a zero is its premise, not an achievement. Ours is measured at `grouped`, BEFORE compose/snap re-seats anything, and SHELL_EPS lifts the floor 0.05 m. Living's 24 break down as 7 ceiling lights, 10 inside the epsilon band, 1 wall-mounted television the prompt calls wall-mounted, and 2 genuine oddities (chair 0.66 m, plant 0.87 m).
- **AND ONE THAT WAS INFLATED:** the intent-vs-placed count mixed furniture with small objects named for their parent ("coffee table_ceramic vase"), which a later step places. Counting them as lost turned a 9-object failure into 17. Now furniture against furniture.
- **THE RESULTS.**
  - living: ours 46 objects / 6 of 7 nouns / 114.9 s (chain minus vote) vs glts 6 objects / 2 of 7 / 53.7 min / 138 calls. Real room 49.73 m2, GLTS guessed 30.0 - **40% too small**.
  - bedroom: ours 82 objects / 15 of 18 nouns / 681 s (partial record) vs glts 9 objects / 7 of 18 / 77.2 min / 164 calls. Real room 22.2 m2, GLTS guessed 21.0 (-5.4%).
  - **GLTS RETRIEVES MORE THAN IT PLACES, ON BOTH SCENES:** living 13 furniture retrieved -> 6 placed (lost the coffee table, television and media console, all named in the prompt); bedroom 13 -> 9. Its weak prompt fidelity is not that it never planned those objects - its own search dropped them.
  - **THE INVENTED ROOM IS NOT ONE DECISION.** GLTS revises it mid-search (bedroom steps 1-3 say 4.2x3.5x3.0, steps 4-13 say 6.0x3.5x3.0). Scoring the step-1 guess would have reported the bed as outside a room it was never placed in.
- **NO COMBINED SCORE AND NO WINNER, DELIBERATELY.** GLTS gets a paragraph; we get a capture of the room the paragraph describes. The report states the trap where it cannot be missed: GLTS optimises directly for the prompt, so when the prompt says "sheer white curtains" and the room has none, we correctly omit them and score WORSE. A lower number may be the method working.
- **OPEN, FOR THE USER:** our bedroom reconstruction shows 62 overlapping object pairs totalling 1.96 m3 (living: 25 pairs, 0.61 m3) with books-in-shelves already excused by the containment rule. That residue is unexplained and wants a look before any of this goes in a paper.

## R-S2-87 - CORRECTION TO R-S2-86: the bedroom GLTS column was contaminated (2026-08-11)
- **WHAT WENT WRONG, MINE.** A bedroom retry was launched while an earlier one was still running - a `;` where I intended `&&`, so a failed syntax check did not stop the launch. Both wrote into output_ovm_bedroom_marble, both exited 0, and the second to finish overwrote the first's run record. The file on disk claimed 157 model calls while I had just reported 164, and the numbered artifacts in between were written by two processes at once. NOTHING ON DISK SAID SO.
- **THE GAP.** glts_run.py isolated by SCENE. The missing rule was ONE WRITER PER OUTPUT ROOT - the same rule scene_lock already enforces for our own pipeline, which the baseline runner never got. Added `paths.dir_lock` (fails fast, names the other pid, breaks a stale lock from a dead process; verified across two real processes) and wired it round every GLTS run. The contaminated directory was moved aside, not deleted, as output_ovm_bedroom_marble_CONTAMINATED_two_concurrent_runs.
- **THE CONTAMINATION WAS NOT COSMETIC.** Clean single run (62.6 min, 172 calls, steps 0-13) against the mixed one: room guess **18.7 m2 (-15.8%)** not 21.0 (-5.4%); prompt nouns **6 of 18** not 7; furniture **15 retrieved -> 9 placed, 8 lost** not 13 -> 9, 4 lost. Every bedroom GLTS figure in R-S2-86 is superseded by the table below.
- **THE CORRECTED RESULTS, both scenes, clean single runs.**
  - **living**: ours 46 objects / 6 of 7 placeable nouns / 114.9 s (chain minus vote, 3 LLM stages) -- glts 6 objects / 2 of 7 / 53.7 min / 138 calls. Real room 49.73 m2 x 3.059 m, GLTS guessed 30.0 m2 x 3.2 m: **area 39.7% too small**. GLTS retrieved 13 furniture and placed 6, losing the coffee table, television and media console, all three named in the prompt.
  - **bedroom**: ours 82 objects / 15 of 18 nouns / 681.4 s (partial record) -- glts 9 objects / 6 of 18 / 62.6 min / 172 calls. Real room 22.2 m2 x 2.764 m, GLTS guessed 18.7 m2 x 3.0 m: **area 15.8% too small**. GLTS retrieved 15 furniture and placed 9, losing the computer monitor, desk lamp and potted plant among others.
  - **THE PATTERN HOLDS ON BOTH SCENES:** GLTS plans more than it places, and what it drops includes objects the prompt names outright. Its low prompt fidelity is its own search discarding planned objects, not a failure to plan them.
- **METHOD NOTE.** This is the third time this session that a process reported success while having done something other than what was claimed - the six no-op flags, GLTS's exit 0 on a dead scene, and now two runs sharing a directory. In every case the individual numbers being printed were true. What caught this one was noticing that a completion notification arrived for a job I had already told the user did not run.

## R-S2-88 - CORRECTION: I LEFT THE PHASE-B2 LOOP-BACK OUT OF THE CHAIN (2026-08-11)
- **USER CAUGHT BOTH.** "I thought I signed off the compose stage back when at designed and ran it? And I thought the pipeline now reruns j1 and j2 before going back to j8, that was the design and that's what have been running no?" Right on both.
- **THE STRUCTURAL ONE.** docs/PLAN_VOTEBOX_DOWNSTREAM.md "PHASE B2" carries a USER ARCHITECTURE RULING of 08-07: "after slice vote the scene goes all the way back up to geometric edges and down the judges again — just with two more judges at the end", and ORDER EXPLICIT: "B2 runs BEFORE the J8/J9 canonical verdicts. The chain is 4g2 re-derive -> J0/J1 (J4/J6 cached) -> THEN J8/J9 at the end", because J8 must read its relational facts from the rebuilt edges (the obj_063 stimulus-gap lesson). Status recorded as IMPLEMENTED + RUN, with real verdicts on the record (chair-merge obj_020 <-> obj_041 SAME .75).
- **graph/stages.py went voted_edges -> j8 directly. The J0/J1 re-run was missing.** I built the table from the 11-step list in docs/AUTOMATION_READINESS.md, which omitted those two stages, and never checked it against the architecture doc that owns the order.
- **THE COST WAS NOT THEORETICAL, AND I MISDIAGNOSED IT AS A DESIGN HOLE.** With J1 never re-run, a SAME_CANDIDATE pair the VOTE created (two chairs, 96% containment) reached materialize with no verdict and shipped as two objects. I wrote that up in R-S2-84/86 as "THE CHAIN HAS NO JUDGE FOR A DUPLICATE THE VOTE ITSELF CREATES" and listed it as an open design question for the user. **That was wrong.** The chain has had a judge for it since 08-07; my table left it out. The gate warning I added is still useful, but its text blamed the design and now names the stage to run instead.
- **FIXED:** `j0_retriage` (triage_pairs --edges-from voted_edges) and `j1_repairs` (judge_pairs --edges-from voted_edges) inserted between voted_edges and j8, with the ruling quoted at the site and the omission recorded there so it cannot be quietly repeated.
- **THE SECOND ONE, ALSO MINE.** I told the user the compose modules were "direction only, not designed" and listed compose sign-off as something they still owed a ruling on. That came from LINE 8 of PLAN_COMPOSE_LOOP.md, written 07-26G at the very start, while the body of the same document records: gate 6 supported_by PASSED 07-31 (re-affirmed via R7), gate 6c consistency PASSED 08-01, R14 S3 add-pass USER PASS "make it canon", R15 S3 v4 built with the user in-session as canon. I quoted the header and ignored the record. What was genuinely missing was the DRIVER, not the design.
- **METHOD NOTE.** Both errors are the same shape: I trusted a summary document over the primary record. AUTOMATION_READINESS's step list was a summary of the chain and was incomplete; PLAN_COMPOSE_LOOP's opening line was a summary of its own status and was stale. In both cases the authoritative answer was further down the same file or in the architecture doc, and in both cases the user knew and I did not check.

## R-S2-89 - THE THREE AUDITS: the chain is right, and it is not the pipeline (2026-08-11, PROVISIONAL)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: the stage tables built earlier today. DECIDES: nothing about any scene - these audits only ask whether what was built matches what was designed. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: a table that runs cleanly and is not the pipeline.
- **THE ANSWER TO "CAN WE RUN 100 FRESH SCENES": NO, AND NOT BY A LITTLE.** Starting from 100 Marble bundles and nothing else, NOTHING runs unattended - not one stage. The scene never reaches the first automated command. Everything built today is real, gated and resumable, and it all begins about twenty hand-run commands in.
- **THE CORE PHASE IS THE WRONG LANE.** run_scene --phase core writes pano_crops/, seg_pano/, scene_manifest_pano.json. NOTHING downstream reads any of them. The chain reads rig_sp0/crops/, rig_sp0/seg_batched20/detections.json, rig_sp0/lift_poolc.json, scene_manifest_pano2c_rc_f30.json. Proof: out/living_marble has NO pano_crops/ and NO seg_pano/ - the core phase has never run on the scene the whole chain was verified against. pipeline_map.html draws the lane that IS used; the runner runs different modules with different names.
- **FOUR GLOBS CRASH ON EVERY REAL BUNDLE.** crop_pano:79, seg_pano_overlay:41, lift_pano:250 glob `*_pano.png`; lift_pano:91 globs `*_collider.glb`. Harvest bundles carry pano_rgb_0.png and collider.glb. **0 of 318 worlds match.** vocab_build was fixed for this on 08-06; these four were not. bedroom_marble works only because its bundle is the deprecated 07-07 manual download.
- **THE REAL CEILING IS 34, NOT 100.** Of 318 harvested worlds: 318 prompts, 318 splats, **34 colliders**, 36 panos, 33 with both. frame_bootstrap refuses without a collider.
- **FLAGS THAT LIVE ONLY IN A PERSON'S MEMORY**, and slicevote hardcodes their results: `pano_lift --suffix c`, `seg_batched --out-dir rig_sp0/seg_batched20`, `manifest_filter --thr 0.30` produce the three filenames slicevote.py:1038-1042 reads at module level with no override. compose/supported_by.py:604 derives the same name with a regex; the chain's FIRST stage does not.
- **A REGRESSION I INTRODUCED TODAY.** run_scene.py:825 `"phase": phase` inside main(), where `phase` is undefined - from commit c9f28f5, a replace that hit three correct sites inside run_graph and one wrong one inside main. It raises NameError exactly when the FINAL GATE FAILS, i.e. on every scene that did not finish, and the exception escapes before log.write() - so **the run log is lost on precisely the runs that need it**, and run_fleet then reads an older run's log instead.
- **THE COMPOSE PASS EARLIER TONIGHT IS CONTAMINATED.** autotest_bedroom is a clone of bedroom_marble and carries five hand-fix files that steer compose, none declared in stages.py, two of them produced by stages not in the tuple at all: snap_rulings.json (pins marked USER_RULING that OUTRANK the model), fit_walk.json (overrides the picks), rotation_check.json (rotates meshes), fit_feedback.json (gates what gets bought), and a prior fitted_preview.json (carries a rotation basis). A fresh scene has none of them and gets a different answer with no crash. Same shape as the --settle-only bug, four times over, softer and therefore worse.
- **AND THE MOOD SHEET IS BLANK.** compose/pick.py:139 builds the room's mood picture from pano_crops/, which the current funnel never creates - so on living_marble the model choosing every asset sees FOUR BLANK WHITE SQUARES, with one printed line as the only signal.
- **THE COMPOSE CHAIN IS WRONG IN THREE INDEPENDENT WAYS**, all quoted canon: fit_check and fit_declip are in the wrong order (rule 8 says preview -> declip -> check, and declip REWRITES fitted_preview in place, so the Collision-Free number - the headline metric - is measured on the un-declipped scene); the fit block is a LOOP ("repeat until dry", a recorded living run took FOUR rounds) and runs once; and two canonized stages are missing (rotation_check PH2a, fit_feedback rule 12). fit_walk as the last row is a guaranteed no-op - it finished in 0.1s on the live run, exactly as predicted.
- **CLEAN, AND WORTH KNOWING:** 142 hits of `obj_0` across graph/, compose/ and the root - **all 142 are comments or examples, zero in a production path**. No hardcoded scene name outside docstrings. No .bak/archive reads. The answer key (gt_labels.json) is scoring-only with the blind-test rule quoted in the code and auto-apply deleted. Every judge cache is a cold-start-safe `if exists else {}`. The --settle-only fix is genuinely fixed.
- **THE J2 QUESTION, ANSWERED:** J2 is NOT part of the Phase-B2 loop-back. The ruling names J0 and J1 only; the merge lands at materialize rule 4. Re-running build_judged would rewrite the immutable `judged` layer and sweep the vote stale. The two stages added in R-S2-88 are right and complete.
- **METHOD, THIRD TIME TODAY:** every one of these was found by checking the PRIMARY record (pipeline_map.html, the owning PLAN doc, the code's own guards) against what was built. None was visible from the summaries, and two of the summaries were themselves wrong.

## R-S2-90 - USER RULING: the map is right, stale things leave the chain (2026-08-11 late)
- **THE RULING, verbatim:** "I think the pipeline viewer in generally correct. Items marked Stale should not be in the core pipeline."
- **SETTLES TWO OPEN QUESTIONS AT ONCE.** (1) The drawn funnel is the real intake lane, so `run_scene --phase core` -- which writes pano_crops/ and seg_pano/ that nothing downstream reads -- is the wrong lane and goes. (2) `voted_edges` is drawn as a TOMBSTONE and therefore leaves the chain.
- **WHAT THE MAP ACTUALLY SAYS, and it is narrower than "delete it":** "RETIRED by this: the graph['vote'] node-sidecar and the graph['voted_edges'] half-layer — FILES STAY ON DISK" and "the 08-07 rederive_voted_edges loop-back and its additive graph['voted_edges'] HALF-layer are retired — edges now follow the nodes INSIDE every whole layer (edge_carry.py). **J0/J1 STILL RUN on the voted layer's edges** as a second pass." So the loop-back survives as a concept; the half-layer does not. The judges keep running and read somewhere else.
- **THE WORK IT IMPLIES,** recorded in the handoff §6b: drop the `voted_edges` row; give triage_pairs/judge_pairs an `--edges-from voted` mode (they offer only record|voted_edges today) and point j0_retriage/j1_repairs at it; do the same for judge_multiplicity, which hard-exits without the block; retire the graph['vote'] block across its four readers (judge_multiplicity:2097, judge_same_product:1038, materialize_layers:274/324, scene_gate:332) while KEEPING vote_doubts.json, which five modules read and which materialize already prefers over the block.
- **WHY IT IS WORTH DOING RATHER THAN TOLERATING:** rederive_voted_edges builds its node set from graph["resolved"]["nodes"] and its boxes from the preview manifest, running BESIDE graph['voted'] — a second source of truth for geometry, which is exactly what R-S2-51 removed everywhere else. It is also the last half-layer in the pipeline, and the whole-layer rule is what makes scene_state trustworthy at all.
- **SWEPT THE MAP FOR EVERYTHING ELSE MARKED RETIRED/STALE/SUPERSEDED/TOMBSTONE.** The other hits are already respected: the old C1-C7 compose chain is not in COMPOSE; `materialize` graduated into materialize_layers.py and the table uses the new one; `resolved` superseded for geometry was fixed in compose today. Nothing else in either table is map-retired.
- **NOTE ON THE RULING'S SHAPE.** It is a general rule, not a one-off: "items marked Stale should not be in the core pipeline." That makes pipeline_map.html the enforceable authority for MEMBERSHIP, not just order — a good rule, because the map is the one artifact that carries retirement history where a reader will see it.

## R-S2-91 - THE HALF-LAYER IS GONE, AND REMOVING IT UNCOVERED A REAL DEFECT (2026-08-11B)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: the user's 08-11 ruling that the map is right and stale things leave the chain, plus the four-part work list in handoff §6b. DECIDES: where the Phase-B2 judges read their edges, and where four modules read the vote's own record. Touches no scene's data. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: a judge that still runs, still reports success, and is now blind to a fact it used to be given.
- **THAT MISTAKE WAS ONE EDIT AWAY, AND THE CHECK CAUGHT IT.** §6b C says point judge_multiplicity at the voted layer's edges. Counted first: `graph['voted_edges']` carried 18 IN_WALL edges on living and 24 on bedroom; the `voted` LAYER carried **zero**. A naive swap would have blinded J8 to every wall relationship in the room, with no crash and no warning.
- **ROOT CAUSE, and it is not about voted_edges at all.** `edge_carry.py:176` built its walls dict as `n["geometry"]["plane"]`, but `build_edges.wall_claim_dist(g, geom)` reads `geom["plane"]` and `geom["extent"]` itself. So it received a plane where it expects a geometry, `plane.get("plane")` was `{}`, and it returned None for **every wall of every node**. The two other callers get it right and say why in a comment (build_edges.py:579, rederive_voted_edges.py:237: "values are the FULL geometry (plane + extent) so wall_claim_dist can guard by segment extent"). A second, smaller bug sat on the same two lines: an `axis in ("x","z")` filter that dropped W5 CONNECTOR segments (axis None) — living's five-segment shell arrived as four.
- **WHAT IT COST, measured on disk before the fix.** edge_carry is how EVERY whole layer derives its edges, so the miss was total: `voted`, `settled`, `shown` and `grouped` each carried **0 IN_WALL edges**, on both scenes, while `resolved` had 19. Nothing looked wrong, because the layers genuinely had no wall edges to be stale. Compose reads `grouped`; a wall-mounted object arriving there with no IN_WALL edge is an object with nothing holding it up.
- **AFTER THE FIX, THE RETIREMENT PROVES ITSELF.** Re-derived each layer in memory and compared pair by pair against `graph['voted_edges']`: autotest_living 85 edges, autotest_bedroom 145, **zero pairs in one and not the other, identical counts of every edge type**. NEAR drops (7->6 living, 6->1 bedroom) are the no-floater fallback correctly retiring, now that those nodes have a real wall edge. So the module is redundant, demonstrated rather than argued.
- **DONE (A-D).** (A) `voted_edges` row dropped from stages.CHAIN; the module stays on disk with a retirement banner at the top of its docstring. (B) New `scene_state.judge_view(graph, layer)` — one accessor giving a judge a layer's nodes (with `label` spelled one way), its own edge list and its nesting; `triage_pairs` and `judge_pairs` take `--edges-from voted`, and the retired spelling refuses with a sentence saying what to use. (C) judge_multiplicity reads the voted layer for both its edges and the vote's record. (D) `graph['vote']` retired across all four readers; `record_vote_doubts` no longer writes the graph at all, and `vote_doubts.json` is now the single copy.
- **A SECOND CRASH CAUGHT THE SAME WAY.** The retired block wrote `tiers: []` and `slice: ""` for every node; build_voted only lifts rule keys that exist, so vote-EXEMPT nodes (kept_wall, kept_ceiling) reach J8 without them — and the docket card does `"→".join(c["tiers"])` unguarded. Living's **obj_018** is exactly that node, exempt and in the docket on a rebox doubt. Defaulted in the adapter. Verified after: the J8 docket built from the voted layer is IDENTICAL to the one built from the retired block on autotest_living (7 cases), autotest_bedroom (21) and living_marble (7), field for field.
- **ONE FEWER SECOND SOURCE OF TRUTH, TWICE.** `materialize_layers.same_verdict_pairs` existed to sweep both blocks because J1's re-judgement landed in one and materialize read the other; with J1 writing into the layer's own edges the split is gone (the `voted_edges` sweep stays, second, only for scenes judged before today). And the members->record evidence walk, previously written out in edge_carry and done a different way in judge_pairs, is now one function: `scene_state.member_evidence`.
- **LIVING_MARBLE WAS NOT TOUCHED.** Its open J9 gate and its stale `grouped` are exactly as they were; the gate run against it is read-only and still reports the same two FAILs it did this morning.

## R-S2-92 - THREE ORDERINGS BECOME REFUSALS, AND J4 COULD NOT RUN ON A POLY-SHELL SCENE (2026-08-11B)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: the ruled judge order (PIPELINE.md:301-312). DECIDES: nothing about any scene — only whether a judge is allowed to start. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: a refusal that fires on a good scene (a stage that legitimately found nothing looking like a stage that never ran), or one that does not fire when the order really is wrong.
- **THE THREE, all previously unenforced while five siblings were.** J0->J1 (judge_pairs), J1->J5 (judge_near), J3->J4 (judge_coherence). Each is silent when reversed rather than loud: J1 without J0 finds a queue and runs happily, having never seen the semantic box-in-box pairs the IoU floor exists to exclude; J5 without J1 folds no SAME verdicts so a duplicate takes two slots on a floater's menu; J4 before J3 caches a verdict keyed on names that are about to change.
- **EVIDENCE, NOT A FLAG.** Each checks the meta block its producer writes — `triage_meta`, `judge_pairs_meta`, `judged.naming_meta` — and all three producers write it BEFORE knowing their own result. So "ran and found nothing" leaves a block with a zero count and "never ran" leaves no block, and the two are distinguishable. No `--force` escape hatch, matching the five that already exist (the only such flag in graph/ is node_evidence's `--allow-holes`, a data-quality gate, not an ordering one).
- **J0->J1 HAS A SECOND LIMB** for a case the block alone cannot see: build_edges rebuilds `graph["edges"]` from scratch, dropping J0's added edges while leaving its meta behind. Fires only when the meta claims nominations and none are on the layer, and the remedy it names is free (triage re-applies from cache with no model calls).
- **SCOPE-CHECKED:** the refusals are layer-scoped, so the record's old run cannot vouch for the `voted` layer's loop-back pass. Verified all four dev scenes carry the evidence on the record, so none false-positives there.
- **AND A REAL DEFECT FOUND ON THE WAY, unrelated to the refusals.** `judge_coherence.build_digest:131` read `plane["value_raw"]` for every arch_wall node and put anything not axis "x" into the z list. A W5 POLYGON shell has one node per SEGMENT, and a CONNECTOR carries kind "connector" with inward_normal_raw/offset_raw, no value_raw and no axis — so it hit the else branch and raised **KeyError. J4 could not run at all on any poly-shell scene, living_marble included.** Invisible because bedroom_marble's shell is four axis walls.
- **SAME SHAPE AS THE edge_carry BUG EARLIER TODAY:** wall-handling code written for the v1 four-plane shell and never revisited for the 08-10 polygon shell. Two in one day, in two different modules, both silent. Worth a sweep of every other `arch_wall` reader.
- **FIXED:** connectors are skipped for the x/z span (a diagonal defines neither) and COUNTED IN THE SENTENCE — "with 1 angled wall segment(s) cutting the corners off that rectangle" — because telling the judge a room is a plain rectangle when a corner is cut off is a worse answer than a longer one. Guarded against a shell with no axis planes at all. Verified: living's digest now builds; bedroom's line is unchanged.

## R-S2-93 - THE FUNNEL IS A TABLE, AND THE DEAD LANE IS OUT (2026-08-11B)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: the user's ruling that the map is right and stale things leave the core pipeline. DECIDES: what `--phase core` IS. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: a funnel that runs and writes its outputs somewhere nothing reads.
- **THE DECISION.** `run_scene --phase core` ran docs/PIPELINE.md's "week8 object-ID lane" — crop_pano into pano_crops/, vocab_from_prompt, seg_views into seg_pano/, seg_pano_overlay, lift_pano into scene_manifest_pano.json, manifest_pano_to_raw. **Nothing downstream reads one of those files**, and out/living_marble — the scene the whole chain was verified against — has neither directory. It now walks `stages.INTAKE`, the lane the map draws and every later stage actually reads. The six modules stay on disk, out of the runner.
- **THE MAP'S COMMAND STRINGS ARE NOT RUNNABLE, and the table is built from the CODE instead.** Three ways they fall short, each checked: `seg_batched`'s `--views-dir` and `--out-dir` are `required=True` and appear in no doc's command line; the map prints `pano_lift` WITHOUT `--suffix` while claiming its c-suffixed outputs at the same node (internally inconsistent — REVIEW_LOG:1557 and the files on disk say `--suffix c`); and the paths are relative.
- **THE RELATIVE PATHS WOULD HAVE BEEN SILENT AND UGLY.** `crop_pano` and `seg_batched` take their directory arguments as a bare `Path(...)` with no scene-dir join, and run_scene spawns every stage with cwd = THE REPO. `--out-dir rig_sp0/crops` would have created `entangled_gen/rig_sp0/crops`, written the scene's crops into the source tree, and let the NEXT scene inherit them. Every path in the table is absolute, built by a helper that says why.
- **THE FLAGS THAT LIVED ONLY IN MEMORY ARE NOW CONSTANTS** at the top of stages.py — RIG, LIFT_SUFFIX, SEG_DIR, DET_BOX_THR, DET_TOPK, LIFT_MIN_SCORE, LIFT_GATE_PEAK, SCORE_THR — with the four canonical filenames DERIVED from them. slicevote.py:1038-1042 and scene_scale.py:159 (a FOURTH hardcode, not previously listed) now have somewhere to import from. Recorded plainly: the "20" in `seg_batched20` is a naming convention for `--box-thr 0.20`, not a derivation; nothing in the code links them.
- **A BUG ONLY A FRESH SCENE COULD SHOW.** `scene_gate.before/after` loaded scene_graph.json unconditionally. The whole funnel runs BEFORE build_graph writes that file, so every intake stage's gate raised "[gate] no scene graph" — **the first genuinely fresh scene would have died at its first stage.** Undiscoverable on a dev scene, because every dev scene already has a graph. The gate now loads it only when a check reads it.
- **`stale_inputs` WALKED `CHAIN` ONLY**, so every `inputs` tuple in COMPOSE was decorative and INTAKE's would have been too. Now walks all three, using each stage's oldest artifact as its timestamp when it writes no layer. It immediately found real staleness on autotest_bedroom — including `rotation_check.json` and `fit_feedback.json`, **two of the five contaminating files handoff §4b warns about**, both about 6.5 days older than their inputs.
- **AND IT NEEDED A WARN, NOT A FAIL, FOR FILE STAGES.** First cut turned the final gate red on both healthy dev scenes. Two of the findings are structural false positives: `scene_scale.py:107` rescales every `scene_manifest_pano*.json` IN PLACE including its own declared input, so its input is ALWAYS newer; and `rotation_check` reads `fitted_preview.json`, which the closing place->jiggle pass rewrites afterwards by design. A layer stage keeps FAIL (its `written_at` is a real stamp on a linear chain); a file stage gets WARN and a message saying it may be a designed loop-back. A gate that is red on every scene is a gate nobody reads.
- **STATE AFTER:** one command now plans 35 stages end to end — 11 intake, 12 graph, 12 compose. The dev scenes still PASS; living_marble still FAILs for the same two pre-existing reasons and was not touched. **The chain still does not START at the beginning: nothing between `shell` and `vote` builds `resolved`.** That is the RECORD half, next.

## R-S2-94 - THE CHAIN NOW STARTS AT THE BEGINNING (2026-08-11B)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: the ruled record/judge order (PIPELINE.md:301-312, PLAN_SCENE_GRAPH.md:234). DECIDES: nothing about any scene — it writes down an order that already existed, so a runner can execute it. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: an order that runs cleanly and is not the ruled one.
- **THE GAP THIS CLOSES IS THE ONE THE HANDOFF CALLED THE HEADLINE.** `CHAIN` began at the vote and read `resolved`; NOTHING in any table built `resolved`. The ten stages from build_graph to materialize_verdicts existed as modules and as prose and in no runnable order, so **a genuinely fresh Marble bundle would run the funnel, reach the vote, and stop.** Both test scenes only ever worked because they are clones that already had that layer.
- **`RECORD` IS ITS OWN TUPLE AND `--phase record` ITS OWN PHASE**, deliberately not appended to `CHAIN`: `--phase graph` has meant "the vote onward" in every handoff and command line, and that meaning is worth keeping.
- **THE ORDER IS COPIED, NOT CHOSEN:** G1 build_graph -> G2 build_edges (stamps `record`) -> J0 -> J1 -> J5 -> J2 build_judged (stamps `judged`) -> J3 names -> J4 coherence -> J6 -> J7 (stamps `resolved`). J3 = names, J4 = coherence, as corrected on 08-11. `judge_cases.py` is NOT wired — its own docstring forbids it.
- **build_graph's THREE PATHS ARE PASSED, NOT DEFAULTED.** Its module defaults hardcode `scene_manifest_pano2c_rc_f30.json` / `rig_sp0/lift_poolc.json` / `rig_sp0/crops` — the same strings a fourth time. The row passes them from the constants at the top of stages.py, so the funnel's parameters and the graph's inputs cannot drift apart.
- **AND THE FLEET DRIVER COULD NOT ASK FOR COMPOSE.** `run_fleet --phase` offered `("core","graph","all")` — no `compose`, and `record` would have been rejected as an invalid choice. That is the driver whose entire purpose is running a hundred scenes unattended, and it could not be asked for a third of the pipeline. Both runners now read one `stages.PHASES_ORDER`, so they cannot drift again.
- **STATE:** one command plans **45 stages** end to end — 11 intake, 10 record+judge, 12 graph, 12 compose. Verified: every RECORD row's declared `reads` passes the gate against a scene that has those layers; both dev scenes still PASS their final gate; living_marble untouched.
- **THIS IS A PLAN, NOT A PASS.** Nothing here has been RUN on a fresh bundle. The next entry is either that run or the reason it did not happen.

## R-S2-95 - A CORRECTION I ALMOST SHIPPED, AND THE PREFLIGHT THAT CANNOT EXIST (2026-08-11B)
- **I NEARLY REPORTED THE GPU CLOCK LOCK AS MISSING, AND THE EVIDENCE DID NOT SUPPORT IT.** Two checks, both inconclusive, and I read both as proof: (1) `nvidia-smi --query-gpu=clocks.max.graphics` returned 3105 MHz — but POWER_CRASHES.md:154 says in terms that nvidia-smi NEVER reports an applied `-lgc` back, and "do not read that as the lock having failed"; (2) `Get-ScheduledTask` did not list `GPUClockLock` — but R-S2-79's follow-up records that the task runs as SYSTEM and a standard user gets access denied on query. Caught before it reached the user, by reading the primary docs instead of trusting my own two commands.
- **THE run_scene WARNING NOW SAYS WHAT CANNOT BE CHECKED AND WHY.** It previously listed the two preconditions as if a reader could verify them; it now states that neither is checkable from an unelevated session, gives the reason for each, and names the only two things that DO prove the lock is on — the receipt at apply time, and `clocks.sm` <= 1500 under load in gpu_watch.csv.
- **THIS IS THE "not yet built" PREFLIGHT OF POWER_CRASHES.md:248**, and it is now clear WHY it cannot be built as specified: there is no queryable state to gate on. An automatic refusal would have to run a GPU burst and watch the clocks, which is the thing it is trying to protect against. The honest version is a loud warning that does not pretend, plus the empirical check in the watcher's log AFTER the fact. Recorded so the next person does not spend the afternoon looking for the field.

## R-S2-96 - THE FIRST GENUINELY FRESH SCENE, AND IT STOPPED AT STAGE ONE (2026-08-11B)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: an unclaimed Marble world (`0a128772-fe76-48c0-8fae-dd612114a0ef`, a bohemian living room) and one command. DECIDES: whether the chain built today actually runs from a raw bundle. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: calling a machinery result a pipeline result, in either direction.
- **THE COMMAND WAS ONE LINE, AND THAT PART IS NEW.** `run_scene.py --scene fresh01 --bundle <uuid>`. It created the scene directory, wrote bundle_path.txt, and planned all 45 stages. Before today this took about twenty hand-typed commands and the scene never reached an automated one.
- **IT FAILED AT `frame`, THE FIRST STAGE, IN 3.9 SECONDS.** `frame_bootstrap` converted the spz to a ply and then REFUSED: "CONTRACT VIOLATION: collider bounds [[-3.31 -1.71 -2.33],[3.86 0.88 5.5]] outside splat bounds [[-3.51 -2.21 -2.78],[4.48 1.39 5.18]] — refusing to guess." The overshoot is on ONE axis (max z, by 0.32 m); the other five are comfortably inside. Note the printed splat bounds ALREADY include the 0.5 m margin, so this is 0.32 m beyond a generous bound.
- **THE MACHINERY DID EXACTLY WHAT IT WAS BUILT TO DO, and that is the honest half of the result.** The refusal was deliberate, not a crash into a traceback; the gate caught it; the runner stopped rather than carrying on into ten stages that would each fail differently; the run log was WRITTEN, with the argv, both gate reports and the timings. That last one matters — the §5f regression destroyed the run log on exactly the runs that failed, and this proves that fix holds.
- **WHAT IT DOES NOT ESTABLISH.** Nothing about the other 44 stages, and nothing about the GPU: the run never reached `stitch`, so the clock-lock question is still open. The watcher was running throughout and logged an idle machine.
- **THE THRESHOLD WAS NOT TOUCHED, ON PURPOSE.** `BOUNDS_MARGIN` is 0.5 and stays 0.5. Loosening a gate because the first test scene tripped it is observation-triggered tuning — Rule #1, and the 08-06 TV-synonym incident is the precedent. A census of all bundles with a collider is running instead, to answer whether this is one bad world or the corpus, and to show whether the pass count is even sensitive to the margin. A flat curve would mean these are real frame mismatches and no margin saves them.
- **OPEN, FOR THE USER:** if most colliders overshoot slightly, the question is whether the trusted-bundle contract is too tight for the current Marble encode, or whether those bundles genuinely cannot be trusted. That is a frame ruling, not a knob, and it is not mine.

## R-S2-97 - THE FRAME CONTRACT REJECTS 20 OF 34 WORLDS, AND NOT FOR THE REASON IT SAYS (2026-08-11B)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: all 34 harvested worlds that have both a collider and an spz. DECIDES: nothing — this is a measurement, and the constant it bears on was NOT changed. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: reading a census as permission to move a gate.
- **HEADLINE: 14 of 34 PASS `frame_bootstrap`'s trusted-bundle contract. 20 FAIL. 0 were unmeasurable** — every spz converted and every glb loaded. Also confirmed: of 323 world folders exactly 34 have a collider, so the corpus is limited far more by colliders missing (289 worlds) than by this gate.
- **THE DIAGNOSIS IS NOT "WRONG FRAME", AND THAT IS THE FINDING.** Measured against the ACTUAL point cloud with NO margin, **33 of the 34 colliders sit entirely inside it.** The frame is right: the collider is centred on the cloud, and its y height matches the splat's y span to within 1% in 27 of 34 worlds. What fails is the comparison, not the bundle.
- **THE 0.5th/99.5th PERCENTILE CROP IS DOING ALMOST ALL THE WORK, not BOUNDS_MARGIN.** The check compares the collider against a cloud with its sparse tails trimmed, so a collider that reaches into a thinly-rendered corner is rejected. The relationship is cleanly monotone in splat density at the offending face: passing worlds have 1,700-68,700 splats within 25 cm of it, failing worlds 0-3,446, and the five worst have 0, 19, 39, 161, 415.
- **VERIFIED INDEPENDENTLY on the world that failed tonight** (`0a128772`): collider entirely inside the true cloud; **1,173 splats within 25 cm of the exact face that was rejected**; FAIL at percentile 0.5, PASS at 0.05 and 0.01 with the margin left at 0.5.
- **BOTH LEVERS, MEASURED.** Margin at fixed 0.5% percentile: 0.5m -> 14, 0.75 -> 17, 1.0 -> 19, 1.5 -> 26, 3.0 -> 30. Percentile at fixed 0.5 m margin: 0.5% -> 14, 0.05% -> 29, 0.01% -> 31. The margin is a real lever and a BAD one — it takes 1.5-3 m to recover the corpus, and a 3 m tolerance no longer detects a wrong frame at all, which is the only thing the check exists for. The percentile recovers the same worlds while leaving the margin tight.
- **THE GATE'S REAL JOB SURVIVES EITHER WAY.** `363c0b4f` is the one genuinely wrong world — its collider reaches 3.83 m above the highest splat in the scene with ZERO splats within 25 cm of that face — and it is caught at every setting tested, including no margin at all.
- **NOTHING WAS CHANGED.** `BOUNDS_MARGIN` is still 0.5 and the percentile is still 0.5/99.5. Moving either after watching a test scene trip them is observation-triggered tuning (Rule #1, the 08-06 precedent), and the fact that the evidence points at the percentile rather than the margin does not make it my call. **USER RULING NEEDED.**
- Two of the 34 are outdoor scenes (`8a62c661` an ancient marketplace street, `6425f0fd` a Lisbon street spanning 263 m) and were probably never corpus candidates; excluding them it is 14 of 32.
- Full per-world data: `scratchpad/frame_contract_survey.json`.

## R-S2-98 - THE FRAME CHECK LOOSENED AT THE RIGHT KNOB, AND THE CATALOGUE NOW SAYS WHAT IS RUNNABLE (2026-08-11B)
- **USER RULING:** "I agree with you. I think the world can mostly be trusted, we can widen the margin." ⚠ APPLIED ON THE PERCENTILE, NOT THE MARGIN, and flagged to the user as such: the recommendation they agreed with was the percentile, and widening the literal `BOUNDS_MARGIN` was the lever measured and advised AGAINST (1.5 m to reach 26 worlds, 3 m to reach 30, and a 3 m tolerance cannot detect a wrong frame at all). Read as "loosen the tolerance"; the user can correct it in one line.
- **CHANGED:** `frame_bootstrap.BOUNDS_TAIL_PCT` 0.5 -> 0.05, a NEW named constant carrying the whole census in its comment. `BOUNDS_MARGIN` is untouched at 0.5.
- **RE-MEASURED AGAINST THE SHIPPED CODE, not the projection.** A sweep that IMPORTS the module's own constants so it cannot drift from them: **29 of 34 PASS, 5 fail, 0 unmeasurable.** Matches the census prediction exactly.
- **THE FIVE THAT STILL FAIL DO SO BY 0.61 to 3.98 m** — 363c0b4f (3.98, the genuinely broken one, collider floating above the scene with zero splats at that face), 8a62c661 (2.82), 6040d57f (2.26), 77eda2e2 (0.81), 4378be67 (0.61). **Nothing fails marginally**, so the check still discriminates rather than having been loosened into uselessness.
- **THE ERROR MESSAGE NOW SAYS HOW BADLY AND ON WHICH AXIS.** It printed two bounding boxes and left the reader to subtract them by eye, on the pipeline's very first stage. Overshoot per axis is exactly what separates a wrong frame (metres, several axes) from a collider grazing a thin corner (centimetres, one axis).
- **THE CATALOGUE NOW ANSWERS "CAN WE RUN IT", which it could not.** It showed `downloaded`, and downloaded is not runnable. `build_catalogue.ps1` folds in `catalog/frame_contract.json`: two new CSV columns (`frame_contract`, `frame_overshoot_m`), three card badges (runnable / frame FAIL +Nm / no collider), three filter buttons, and a summary section. Blank means NOT MEASURED and is deliberately distinct from `fail`.
- **AND IT MAKES THE REAL CEILING PLAIN: 29 runnable, 5 blocked, 284 downloaded with NO COLLIDER AT ALL.** The collider, not this check, is what limits the corpus — 34 of 318. That was always in the numbers and was never on the page.
- **ENCODING TRAP, worth knowing:** PowerShell 5.1 reads a BOM-less `.ps1` as ANSI, so any non-ASCII in a here-string reaches the output file double-encoded — the first build emitted mojibake em-dashes into the summary. The generator is now pure ASCII.

## R-S2-99 - THE FUNNEL RAN. ALL ELEVEN STAGES, FROM A RAW BUNDLE, UNATTENDED (2026-08-11B)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: `188a1d3f`, a rustic attic bedroom that had never been downloaded into a scene. DECIDES: whether the intake funnel actually works from one command. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: counting a clone's result as a fresh scene's.
- **IT RAN. 11 of 11 intake stages PASSED, gates green on both sides of every one, 872.7 s from `run_scene.py --scene fresh02 --bundle <uuid>`.** This morning that was about twenty hand-typed commands and the scene never reached an automated one.
```
frame 2.9s  stitch 37.2s  crops 8.1s  vocab 293.5s  bearings 26.7s
detect 180.7s  lift 7.0s  recenter 208.4s  filter 0.1s  scale 106.4s  shell 1.7s
```
- **WHERE THE TIME GOES, on a real scene:** vocab 33.6%, recenter 23.9%, detect 20.7%, scale 12.2%. **Two thirds of the funnel is model calls, not GPU.** vocab is the single most expensive stage in the funnel and it is UNCACHED (~6 haiku calls, vocab_build.py) — the obvious target if 100 scenes need to be cheaper.
- **THE GPU LOCK IS ON, MEASURED AT LAST.** Sampled through the `detect` burst: 16 samples above 30% utilisation, **peak exactly 1500 MHz, 89.6 W** — against POWER_CRASHES.md's locked 93.8 W and the ~190 W transients seen unlocked. R-S2-95 said the receipt or the load log were the only proofs; this is the load log.
- **IT THEN CRASHED AT `build_graph`, THE FIRST STAGE AFTER THE FUNNEL:** `[record] MISSING inputs: ['envelope']`.
- **AND THE MAP WAS RIGHT AGAIN.** `build_graph.load_inputs` demanded `envelope.npz` unconditionally, but reads it at exactly ONE site: the ELSE branch that builds PLACEHOLDER architecture nodes when there is no room_shell.json. room_shell superseded that on 07-26, envelope.py was PARKED the same day, and the map has drawn it with no outgoing arrow ever since. So the funnel correctly does not run it — and a stale precondition then killed the first fresh scene at the first stage after eleven passes. **Every dev scene has a leftover envelope.npz from the era when it did run, which is exactly why nothing caught this in months.**
- **FIXED AT THE SOURCE, scene-agnostically:** `envelope` is required only when `room_shell.json` is absent, and the placeholder branch now refuses with a sentence naming `room_shell.py` and stating that envelope.py is parked and is not the fix.
- **THIS IS THE THIRD DEFECT TODAY OF THE SAME SHAPE** — code that works on every dev scene because every dev scene carries an artifact the current pipeline no longer produces. The others: `scene_gate` loading a graph the funnel has not written yet, and `pick`'s mood sheet reading the retired lane's crop directory. A clone cannot find any of them.

## R-S2-100 - THE RECORD HALF RAN TOO. RAW BUNDLE -> `resolved`, UNATTENDED (2026-08-11B)
- **ALL 10 RECORD/JUDGE STAGES PASSED, 345.6 s**, resumed with `run_scene.py --scene fresh02 --phase record` after the envelope fix. Together with the funnel that is **21 of 45 stages, from a raw Marble bundle, with no human in the loop** — and it is precisely the half the 08-25B handoff said "no script contains".
- **THE LAYER CHAIN BUILT CLEANLY:** record 52 nodes / 102 edges -> judged 37 / 85 -> resolved 31 / 64. J7 removed 6 with reasons recorded. `scene_state.check()` agrees, nothing stale.
- **THE THREE NEW ORDER REFUSALS DID NOT FALSE-POSITIVE** on a real chain run in the ruled order — J0->J1->J5 and J3->J4 all passed through. That is the check that mattered for them: they were written against dev scenes that already had every artifact, so a fresh scene running in order was the first honest test.
- **IN_WALL CARRIED: 29 -> 27 -> 23** down the three layers. This is the edge type `edge_carry` was silently dropping from `voted` onward until this morning; on the existing scenes the fix could only be verified in memory, because their layers on disk still hold zero. Whether the 23 survive INTO `voted` is the first on-disk proof, and the vote is running now.

## R-S2-101 - THE PROOF: 31 IN_WALL EDGES IN `voted` ON A SCENE THAT NEVER KNEW THE OLD CODE (2026-08-11B)
- **THE edge_carry FIX IS CONFIRMED ON DISK.** fresh02's `voted` layer carries **31 IN_WALL edges** (resolved had 23). **Before this morning's fix that number would have been 0** — every layer from `voted` onward, on every scene, silently. It is 31 rather than 23 because the vote MOVED boxes and more objects now touch a wall, which is the re-derivation working rather than edges merely being copied across.
- **WHY THIS IS THE ONLY REAL EVIDENCE.** On living and bedroom the fix could be checked only in memory: their layers on disk still hold zero and will until their chains are re-run. fresh02 was built after the fix, start to finish, so its 31 were never anything else.
- **BOTH RETIREMENTS CONFIRMED THE SAME WAY.** On this scene `graph["voted_edges"]` is ABSENT and `graph["vote"]` is ABSENT — the half-layer and the node-sidecar are genuinely out of the chain, not merely unused. And nothing needed them: all 31 voted nodes carry their own vote record and their own doubts, inside the layer, which is exactly what §6b said should replace them.
- **`voted` also carries 13 nesting facts and 100 edges** including 3 SAME_CANDIDATE for the loop-back judges to answer. The whole-layer contract holds on a scene built entirely under it.

## R-S2-102 - A FIFTH READER OF THE RETIRED BLOCK, AND A FALSE POSITIVE I SHIPPED THIS MORNING (2026-08-11B)
- **THE GRAPH CHAIN GOT SIX STAGES IN AND CRASHED AT `j8s`:** `[splitcuts] no graph['voted_edges']`. vote (185.3 s), doubts, voted, j0_retriage (19.5 s), j1_repairs (13.1 s) and j8 all PASSED first.
- **`split_cuts.py` IS A FIFTH READER OF THE RETIRED HALF-LAYER.** §6b's work list named four (judge_multiplicity, judge_same_product, materialize_layers, scene_gate) and I confirmed against a grep I had TRUNCATED WITH `head -20`. A complete sweep finds split_cuts:1684 (hard exit — the crash), scene_gate:474 (benign, iterates both) and viewer/serve.py:402. **Same lesson as R-S2-88, and this time the summary I over-trusted was my own truncated output.** All fixed; the retired block is still read SECOND everywhere, for scenes voted before today.
- **AND THE GATE TOLD THE OPERATOR TO CORRUPT THE SCENE.** The `stale_inputs` extension I added this morning reported: "`judged` was built 9 min BEFORE its input graph/judge_pairs_cache.json — re-run `build_judged` and everything after it." Both facts true, conclusion WRONG. The Phase-B2 loop-back re-runs J1 after the vote and rewrites that cache ON PURPOSE, and AUTOMATION_READINESS.md:301-304 says in terms: "J2 is NOT part of the loop-back ... re-running build_judged would rewrite the immutable `judged` layer and sweep the vote stale." **A check whose remedy corrupts the scene is worse than no check.**
- **FIXED STRUCTURALLY, not with a special case.** An input that a LATER stage declares as its own artifact is a designed loop-back, and is skipped. Built from the tables, so there is no exception list to keep in step. Required giving `j0_retriage` and `j1_repairs` the artifacts they write — which they had NEVER declared, so those two LLM stages also had no no-op protection at all until now. Two holes, one fix.
- **VERIFIED:** fresh02 now reports only its two TRUE failures (ended on `voted`, no `shown` layer); both dev scenes still PASS.
- **RUNNING TALLY OF DEFECTS ONLY A FRESH SCENE COULD FIND: five.** edge_carry's silent IN_WALL loss, scene_gate loading a graph the funnel has not written, pick's mood sheet on the retired lane, build_graph's stale envelope precondition, split_cuts' retired-block requirement — plus this false positive, which a fresh scene caught within minutes of my writing it.

## R-S2-103 - A STAGE THAT FAILED AFTER IT HAD ALREADY SUCCEEDED (2026-08-11B)
- **`evidence` CRASHED WITH ITS LAYER ALREADY WRITTEN.** The log reads: "layer `shown`: 28 node(s) with a picture, 0 problem(s)" -> "wrote graph['shown']" -> "additive check: PASS" -> Traceback. The gate then confirmed "evidence layer whole: 28/28 nodes have a picture". The stage did its job, stamped it, and then died BUILDING THE HUMAN REVIEW PAGE.
- **ROOT CAUSE, scene-agnostic:** `node_evidence.recut_rect` clamped each edge of the crop rectangle INDEPENDENTLY — `max(0, left) … min(w, right)`. That is fine while the box overlaps the photo and INVERTS when it does not: a box entirely off the left gives left=0 and a negative right; entirely off the right gives left>w and right=w. PIL raises "Coordinate 'right' is less than 'left'". A box projecting off-frame is ORDINARY, not exotic — the aimed view that best frames one object need not contain another.
- **FIXED** by clamping each edge INTO the photo, which makes an off-frame box DEGENERATE (zero width or height) rather than inverted — and the caller's existing `width > 3 and height > 3` guard already skips those. Tested against seven cases (inside, off each of the four sides, straddling, larger than the photo): all valid, PIL accepts every one, and the in-frame result is byte-identical to before.
- **THE CLASS IS WORTH MORE THAN THE INSTANCE, AND IT IS NOT FIXED.** A REVIEW ARTIFACT took down a stage whose real output was already committed. On a hundred unattended scenes any such crash costs a scene that had actually succeeded, and leaves an incoherent state: the runner says CRASH, the gate says the layer is whole, and both are right. Whether `write_report`-style builders should be allowed to fail their stage is a POLICY question, not a bug — wrapping them changes what "a stage failed" means. **NOT DECIDED HERE. Flagged for the user.**
- **SIX defects now that only a fresh scene could find**, and this one is the first of a new kind: not a stale artifact every dev scene happens to carry, but a geometric edge case no curated scene had produced.

## R-S2-104 - ⭐ THE MEASUREMENT HALF IS DONE: RAW BUNDLE -> `grouped`, FINAL GATE PASS (2026-08-11B)
- **`[gate] PASS: final state of fresh02`.** Ended on `grouped`, no stale layers, evidence layer whole 28/28. **33 of 45 stages — the entire measurement half — from a Marble bundle that had never been downloaded into a scene, with no human in the loop.**
- **THE WHOLE LAYER CHAIN, EVERY LAYER WHOLE:**
```
record   52 nodes  102 edges  IN_WALL 29
judged   37         85                27
resolved 31         64                23
voted    31        101                31
settled  28         77                27
shown    28         77                27
grouped  28         82                27      (+5 SAME_PRODUCT from J9)
```
- **READ THE IN_WALL COLUMN.** Every layer carries wall edges. **Before this morning every number from `voted` down would have been 0** — silently, on every scene the project has ever built. This scene has never contained the broken code, so the column is not a repair, it is what the pipeline produces when it is right.
- **BOTH RETIREMENTS HOLD ON A SCENE BUILT ENTIRELY UNDER THEM:** `graph['voted_edges']` and `graph['vote']` are absent, and nothing wanted them.
- **THIS IS THE CLAIM THE 08-25B HANDOFF SAID COULD NOT BE MADE**, and it can now be made in the exact form that handoff demanded: backed by a scene that had never run before. What it does NOT yet cover is compose — 12 stages, running now.
- **THE HONEST COST, from the run logs:** intake 872.7 s, record+judge 345.6 s, graph chain ~330 s of stage time, plus six fix-and-resume cycles that a clean run would not pay. Call it ~26 minutes of machine time per scene for the measurement half, of which the single most expensive stage is `vocab` at 293 s and UNCACHED.

## R-S2-105 - THE FUNNEL'S MOST EXPENSIVE STAGE: 3m33s -> 3.0s, AND IT IS NOW REPRODUCIBLE (2026-08-11B)
- **USER RULING:** "cache the vocab." And: "if the review page fails but job is actually complete its ok to continue, but try to find out why and fix."
- **WHY `vocab` COST 293 s.** Measured, not guessed: a bare claude.exe round trip is ~3 s, so process overhead is ~9 s of it and the rest is the model thinking across FOUR calls. `vocab_build.py` contained **zero** occurrences of the word "cache" — every re-run paid the full cost for the same answer, and fresh02's funnel was re-run four times today.
- **TWO CACHES, BECAUSE THERE ARE TWO KINDS OF QUESTION.** Three of the four legs — concreteness, detector synonyms, the "image-word" screen (text-only despite its name) — are pure functions of a WORD LIST and have nothing to do with the room. Shared across every scene, **keyed PER TERM**. The fourth reads this room's photograph: per-scene, keyed on the image's content hash.
- **PER-TERM IS THE LOAD-BEARING CHOICE, and it is measured.** Across the three genuinely distinct rooms on disk, 31-48% of terms are shared and 66% of all distinct terms appear in more than one room. A whole-list key — which is what scene_scale.py uses, the obvious thing to copy — would hit almost never, because no two rooms have the same word list.
- **RESULT: 3m33s -> 3.0 s, 4 model calls -> 0.** Verified correct the only way that counts: two consecutive warm runs are BYTE-IDENTICAL including the gdino query string.
- **AND A REAL FINDING FELL OUT OF THE VERIFICATION.** My first comparison showed a difference and I nearly called the cache broken. It was not: `prompt_raw` and `frames_raw` were identical and the whole difference was in `pano_raw`. **The pano look-pass is materially NON-DETERMINISTIC** — two runs over the SAME image returned `crate, ladder, rug, trunk` vs `box, chair, door, lantern, window`, on a 9-term answer. So before today, re-running a scene could give it a different vocabulary and therefore different detections. **The cache does not merely speed this stage up; it makes it reproducible.**
- **PROMPT_VERSION added** and salted into every key, so an edited prompt re-asks rather than serving an answer to a question no longer being posed — the same rule judge_pairs and triage_pairs already use. `vlm_calls` also fixed: the concreteness leg never counted itself, so the recorded "3" was really 4.
- **THE REVIEW-ARTIFACT RULING IS IMPLEMENTED** as `paths.report_guard`: it runs a picture-builder, and on failure prints the full traceback loudly, writes a `report_failures.json` marker, and lets the stage continue. `scene_gate.quality_notes` surfaces that marker as a WARN on every report, so "continue" cannot become "ignore". ⚠ Its docstring states the narrow scope in terms — only a step that draws something for a human, only AFTER the real output is committed — because this repo's recurring injury is a stage reporting success having done nothing, and a swallowed exception is how that happens. Wired at exactly one site: node_evidence, after write_layer.

## R-S2-107 - USER RULINGS ON THE OPEN ISSUES, AND THE CORE ONE FIXED (2026-08-11B)
- **RULINGS TAKEN.** `prep_scene` -> make it a stage. `support_clip` -> RETIRE ("if we already have a similar mechanism... its safe to retire if most of the docs say its retired"). `fit_feedback` -> do NOT build the re-shop ("shopping is highly dependent on the asset library quality which is out of scope; I just need to prove our pipeline is rich and functional") — read back to the user for correction. Paper metric -> decide after a batch works. `grouped`/`shown` -> FIX, "a core function". Slanted walls, ctop, J9 -> defer. Global vocab dictionary -> APPROVED.
- **⭐ THE CORE FIX: `grouped` NOW INHERITS `shown`.** Confirmed on fresh02 BEFORE the fix: the `shown` block was on 28 of 28 nodes in `shown` and **0 of 28 in `grouped`**. `grouped` is the final layer and the one compose reads, so the pipeline decided which picture represents each object and then threw the answer away on the last step. Contradicts the user's own layer rule of 08-08 ("each module is an edit... it has to inherit all the properties and information"). `materialize_layers.inherit_shown()` carries it by id; after: **28 inherited, 0 missing**, recorded in `counts.shown_inherited` / `shown_missing` so a split piece with no picture is counted rather than assumed away.
- **`prep_viewer` IS NOW THE LAST COMPOSE STAGE**, and the omission it fixes is a good example of the class: a scene could pass all 45 stages and every gate and still be **invisible** — the viewer lists `viewer/data/*.bin` and nothing in the pipeline wrote there. fresh02 finished completely and was absent from the dropdown. Last in the tuple, no model calls, no GPU, `--skip prep_viewer` for runs nobody will look at (~19.5 MB/scene, ~2 GB per hundred).
- **THAT REQUIRED A REAL GATE EXTENSION, not a fudge.** The viewer payload lives in the REPO and `out/` is not inside the repo, so no relative path from the scene dir reaches it. `scene_gate.artifact_path()` now understands a `{scene}` placeholder and a `repo:` prefix, and all four resolution sites go through it. Without that, `prep_viewer` could declare no artifact at all — and a stage with no declared artifact is invisible to the no-op trap, which is the one thing the gate exists for. Verified: PASS on fresh02 (payload present), FAIL on autotest_living (absent).
- **THE CORPUS PAGE THE USER ASKED FOR:** `catalog/CORPUS_REVIEW.html`, 435 cards. Part 1, what we hold: **29 runnable · 5 frame mismatch · 284 NO COLLIDER** of 318. Part 2, what we skipped: 52 DROP + 65 unsure, each showing the exact keywords that decided it. The triage rule is `filter.ps1`: DROP if it looks like a panorama, or hits an outdoor word (street/forest/mountain/cityscape) and NO indoor word — so an interior described by its view ("a cabin overlooking mountains") is dropped and the rule cannot tell. That is what needs the user's eye.
- **AND THE SCENE THE PIPELINE PROVED ITSELF ON IS A BAD QUALITY SPECIMEN, by the user's read and confirmed by measurement.** fresh02 is an attic with a PITCHED roof; the shell fits only vertical planes and one flat ceiling, so it concluded the room is **1.64 m tall everywhere**. That propagated: scene_scale saw three "ceiling" observations at ratios 0.33/0.41/0.47 against a 2.8 m prior, spread 0.42, and **degraded to scale 1.0** — which R-S2-99 reported as a clean degrade without connecting it to the roof. The machinery result stands; the geometry does not. **Next test scene should be a plain box-shaped room.**

Per scene, at the call_claude boundary, keyed md5(PROMPT_VERSION | model | full prompt). Sound HERE because the round loop folds every earlier proposal into the next prompt, so an identical prompt implies identical history; failures are never stored, and the firm-retry sends a different prompt so a malformed answer is retried under a different key. A fleet launched overnight with a typo would fail every scene identically. Now validated at launch against stages.BY_KEY, with the four stage tables printed in the refusal.
