## R-S2-112 - fresh03: 33 STAGES CLEAN, THEN THE CONNECTOR BIT ITS THIRD AND FOURTH READERS (2026-08-11C)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: fresh03, world 44205719 (a contemporary gray bedroom, never run), one command, all 46 stages. DECIDES: whether the pipeline completes uninterrupted on a fresh world. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: calling a resumed run the proof, or fixing the crash for this scene instead of for every scene.
- **THE RUN: intake 11/11, record 10/10, graph 12/12 - 33 stages, gates green on both sides of every one, 2205 s, from ONE command with zero intervention.** The graph-half final gate PASSes: ended on `grouped`, no stale layers, 23/23 nodes pictured, 5/30 slice fallbacks (PARKED #1 reported, not touched). GPU clock lock held: 227 loaded samples, peak EXACTLY 1500 MHz / 101 W.
- **THEN `supported_by` CRASHED IN 0.1 s** - `KeyError: None` at wall_gap: `{"x":0,"z":2}[plane["axis"]]`. fresh03's shell has a CONNECTOR segment (the polygon fit cutting a corner; axis=None, kind='connector'), and supported_by assumed every wall is an axis plane.
- **THIS IS THE KNOWN DEFECT CLASS, THIRD INSTANCE** - stages.py's shell-row note says it verbatim: edge_carry and judge_coherence made the same assumption and were fixed 2026-08-11; "anything new reading room_shell.json must handle kind=='connector'". fresh02 (a rectangle) could not catch it; the whole compose chain had simply never met a connector. Another defect only a fresh scene could find.
- **FIXED AT THE SOURCE, SCENE-AGNOSTICALLY, both readers:**
  - `compose/supported_by.py` wall_gap: connector branch measures the signed gap along the segment's inward normal over the box's four plan corners (n.p - offset, interior positive, room_shell's stated convention), deepest corner keeps the sign contract; face reported 'nearest-corner'.
  - `compose/snap.py` (the FOURTH reader, found by sweeping compose for the same pattern BEFORE resuming): snapping flush to a connector slides the box along the inward normal until the deepest plan corner sits on the wall line. Same disposition, axis walls untouched.
  - fit_preview/propose_edits already guard (they skip planes with no value_raw by construction); prep_scene, consistency and the fit block do not read wall axes at all - swept, not assumed.
- **RESUMING fresh03 from compose.** Per the standing bar: a resumed run is NOT the proof - the next fresh world must run clean end to end. That next world is fresh04 (0f874584, colliderless), which now carries BOTH proofs: the uninterrupted bar and the collider-optional path.

- **CONTRACT.** GETS: any scene graph. DECIDES: the content fingerprint compose layers stamp and the viewer staleness gate compares. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: a formula change that silently re-fingerprints EXISTING scenes - every compose layer everywhere would flip to stale at once.

MATCH - the formula did not move for axis walls); fresh03 fingerprints without raising; autotest_living MISMATCHES its stamp and that is CORRECT - its stamp predates the W5 wall migration (the old formula could not fingerprint its current 5-segment graph at all), so the staleness the viewer gate reports there is real and pre-existing.

## R-S2-115 - DECLIP OBJECTIVE REFINED BY USER RULING: CLIPPING ALLOWED, BUT MINIMAL - PAIRED OR NOT (2026-08-11C)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: the placed preview after the equilibrium loop. DECIDES: how far every surviving clip can be reduced within the standing locks (wall-normal, dual-attachment, hug, shell). A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: tearing layered wall dressing apart in the name of zero collision, or calling an equilibrium stall "minimal".
- **USER RULINGS (in session, on fresh04's preview):** first "the goal priority is to have no collision" with minimise-translation demoted to a criteria; then refined when offered the layered-pair fork: **"the clipping should be minimal, its allowed. but its not because its paired, even paired mesh should be minimally clipped."** So: no zero-collision teardown, no paired exemptions - every pair ground to its constrained minimum.
- **BUILT: a minimal-clip pass after the main loop** (compose/fit_declip.py). Deepest pair first, single 2 cm lattice steps of either member along UNLOCKED axes. First version used a strict no-worsening third-party guard - and froze whole wall rows solid (packed rows: any slide briefly deepens a neighbour). Replaced with **NET-TOTAL acceptance**: a step is kept when the mover's total intersection cells strictly fall, so the door may hand overlap to the panel while the sum drops; sweeps re-rank pairs so the hand-off gets its own turn. Strictly decreasing integer total -> terminates; caps: 40 steps/pair/sweep, 8 sweeps, 600 total.
- **MEASURED on a throwaway clone of fresh04** (the real scene untouched; clone deleted): **21 clips / 66.0 L -> 18 clips / 44.3 L (-33%)**. door x door 745 -> 6 cells; curtain x curtain 356 -> 5; the worst pair (closet door x window) 2139 -> 1633 (-24%) and then a TRUE bind - that wall row is over-subscribed, so every slide loses as much as it gains. The remainder is a re-shop or upstream-dedup question, not a solver stall. Runtime +~3 s on a 129 s stage.
- The locks THEMSELVES were not touched: wall-normal, dual-attachment (door), hug and shell constraints all stand; rotation eligibility unchanged. The ruling changed the OBJECTIVE, not the physics.
- Applying to fresh04 via the runner (--phase compose --from fit_preview), so the user's preview reflects the canon.

## R-S2-116 - THE HUG LOCK WAS FREEZING MOVES *TOWARD* THE WALL - THE USER SAW IT WITH THE NAKED EYE (2026-08-11C)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: the declip solver's movement rules for floor furniture near walls. DECIDES: which directions a wall-hugging item may slide. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: a rule meant as "do not drift off your wall" implemented as "do not move on that axis at all".
- **THE USER, looking at fresh04's preview: "the wardrobe can go -z direction, and everything else can too... was that allowed? why was that not done?"** The diagnostic proved them right on the numbers: obj_023 (wardrobe) had 0.18 m of genuinely free space at -z and the hug lock had frozen its whole z axis - both directions, including sliding CLOSER to the hugged wall. The convoy move the user proposed was legal physics blocked by one over-broad rule.
- **FIXED: the hug is now DIRECTIONAL** (compose/fit_declip.py, `hug` map replacing the axis entry in `lock`): the sign pointing AT the hugged wall is allowed (the shell bound stops the item at the wall itself), the sign away from the wall stays forbidden - that drift is what the hug exists to prevent. Shell push-back bypasses it exactly as before. Wall items' plane locks and the door rule untouched.
- **MEASURED, the honest way after a false start:** re-declipping the already-declipped scene compounded moves and looked WORSE (52.3 L) - a contaminated comparison. From the naive placement, as the pipeline actually runs: **17 clips / 41.3 L vs the shipped 18 / 47.7 L**, the door x door pair fully resolved (899 -> 0 cells), bed x curtain resolved (137 -> 0), and the over-subscribed closet-door x window bind down another third (2065 -> 1395 cells). 23 items moved, 5 rotated.
- Clone deleted; applying to fresh04 through the runner.

## R-S2-117 - THE ALLOWED CLIPPING MARGIN: ASSET JUNK STAYS, THE NUMBERS STAY HONEST (2026-08-11C)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: every pairwise mesh overlap in the placed preview. DECIDES: which overlaps count as clips (worked and reported) and which are acceptable touches. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: hiding overlaps (they must stay recorded), or a margin tuned by anything other than the user's eye.
- **USER RULINGS:** "i dont want to fix the asset library. so lets just have good margins for clipping. i think visually as long as its not obvious it will be ok" - after spotting a broken mesh sliver on the wardrobe asset (89888d17249b47789c401a1c40a3c36f) protruding toward the -z wall. Slight clips from junk geometry are allowed by design, not defects to chase.
- **BUILT:** `fit_check.ALLOW_L = 0.5` (62 cells at 2 cm) - a NAMED margin whose comment carries the ruling. fit_check splits pairs: above margin -> `clips` (reported, worked), at/below -> `contacts` (RECORDED in the json, never hidden - record-then-judge). fit_declip imports the margin and leaves at-or-below-margin pairs alone at all six decision sites (pair collection, rotation clearing + worse-check, mc pass entry + loop, residuals). fit_walk needs no edit - it reads `clips` and so stops swapping assets over allowed touches.
- **THE VALUE IS A STARTING POINT, NOT A CALIBRATION.** 0.5 L was chosen as "roughly a fist"; whether it matches "visually not obvious" is the user's judgement to render after looking at the re-run preview. It moves on their say-so only (the R-S2-97 lesson: measure, then ask - here the instrument is their eye).
- Applies to fresh04 on the next fit-block run (one was already in flight with only the R-S2-116 hug fix; a follow-up picks this up).

## R-S2-118 - WALL-BACKED FURNITURE MAY NOT ROTATE: THE 15-DEGREE WARDROBE (2026-08-11C)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: rotation eligibility in the declip solver. DECIDES: which items may take a yaw as a de-clipping move. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: a wardrobe with its back flat against a wall standing at 15 degrees to it.
- **THE USER, on the re-run preview: "the wardrobe is now rotated which is super weird... everything else improved."** Their guessed mechanism was inverted - wall-MOUNTED items already cannot rotate; the wardrobe is floor-mounted, so it COULD, and once R-S2-116 unfroze its slide the rotation-first rule found a 15-degree yaw that cleared cells and took it. A bedside table took 10 degrees the same way.
- **THE GAP:** may_rotate() checked mount and flatness but never the HUG. The module's own principle - "a wall item's yaw is what holds it flat against its wall" - applies verbatim to a floor item BACKED against a wall: the yaw is what keeps its back flat, and any rotation digs a corner into the wall.
- **FIXED:** hug-locked items are no longer rotation-eligible. Tucked items (observed facing the wall - the desk-chair case) carry no hug entry and keep their freedom, exactly as before. One line plus the reasoning in the docstring.
- Re-running the fit block; this is the fourth user-eye finding of the evening (R-S2-115..118) and the pattern holds: the machinery is sound, the RULES meet the eye one case at a time.

## R-S2-119 - THE FLOOR-PARKED SMOKE DETECTOR AND THE BED ALLOWED OFF ITS WALL (2026-08-11C)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: add-proposal placement (propose_edits) and the hug allowance (fit_declip). DECIDES: which surface an invented item lives on, and how far wall-hugging furniture may drift to de-clip. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: a ceiling device on the floor, or a bed forbidden to clear a window frame by centimeters.
- **USER FINDINGS on fresh04 (two more eye-catches):** the smoke detector "mad clipping" the stool - because the add proposal SAID where="ceiling, away from ceiling light" but emitted support="floor", and placement trusted support verbatim: a ceiling device parked on the floor. And bed x window (4 L): the bed belongs on that wall; the window asset protrudes; the bed was forbidden to give an inch.
- **USER RULINGS:** "you are right about the bullshit smoke detector" + "i think we can allow the bed to be dragged off the wall a bit. lets fix those two things."
- **FIX 1 (propose_edits):** surface reconciliation before dispatch - when the FIRST word of the proposal's own `where` names an arch surface (ceiling/wall/floor) and `support` names a different one, the where-surface wins; the original is kept as `support_as_proposed`. Plain word match, no inference, object supports never overridden. Plus the missing `support == "ceiling"` placement branch: flush under the ceiling at a free spot, ceiling-height items as obstacles (so the add does not stack on the ceiling light), up-sign aware. shopping already maps support ceiling -> mount ceiling (shopping.py:202), so nothing downstream needed.
- **FIX 2 (fit_declip):** the hug's away-from-wall direction gets a BOUNDED allowance instead of zero: an item may drift off its hugged wall as far as de-clipping demands but must still END within HUG_M of it. Hugging preserved; "flush" stopped being mandatory. NO NEW CONSTANT - HUG_M already defines hugging distance.
- Re-running fresh04 from propose_edits (cached LLM answers; the placement code re-executes on them).

## R-S2-119b - THE OFF-WALL DRIFT CAPPED AT 0.2 m, USER-SET (2026-08-11C)
- USER, after seeing the bed give more room than expected: "i also think the allowed margin for the bed move is too much, maybe like 0.2 m lol. i think this is arbitrary but lets just do that for now." New named constant `HUG_DRIFT_M = 0.20` in fit_declip (was implicitly HUG_M = 0.30), comment carries the ruling verbatim. Second eye-calibrated knob alongside fit_check.ALLOW_L; both move on the user's say-so only.

## R-S2-120 - TRIMMED SCALE CONSENSUS + HEAD-WORD SHOPPING: THE 0.66x ROOM GETS NORMALIZED (2026-08-11C)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: the scale rulers (object sizes vs typical-size priors) and the shopping category matcher. DECIDES: whether the scene is rescaled to true meters, and which asset aisle a label shops in. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: a correct 0.66 consensus refused because a mirror wearing a "wall molding" badge voted 10.8x - or an accent table shopping among serving trays.
- **THE USER'S CALL: "key problem that might fix a lot of things. the scene is not normalized in size."** Right on the consequence chain: shopping fits METRIC assets into NON-metric boxes, so at 0.66 scale a side table's box reads tray-sized, scale-penalty scoring misfires, and clip weirdness follows.
- **DIAGNOSIS from fresh04's ruler table:** the measurement was RIGHT (six doors 0.58-0.70, wardrobes 0.82, wall 0.58 -> median 0.66) and the honesty gate refused anyway (rel-MAD 0.25 > 0.2) because five broken rulers voted: the molding-that-is-a-mirror at 10.8x, truncated stool/bench boxes at 0.19-0.31, one mislabel at 1.48.
- **FIX 1 (scene_scale): TRIMMED CONSENSUS** - rulers disagreeing with the median by more than TRIM_X=2.0 in either direction are dropped BEFORE the agreement check (a 10x "ruler" is a mislabel, not evidence); MIN_N and MAX_SPREAD unchanged and now judge the survivors; trimmed ids are printed and recorded (n_used/n_trimmed/trimmed_ids), never hidden.
- **FIX 2 (retrieve2.match_categories): HEAD-WORD PREFERENCE** - English compounds are head-final; within each match tier, categories containing the label's LAST word win, modifier matches survive only when nothing matches the head. Verified: "small tray table" -> ["table"] (was ["tray"], whose shortlist included a tray of bread with turkey); "floor lamp" unchanged; "rug" was and remains LLM-mapped.
- Also riding along: HUG_DRIFT_M = 0.20 (R-S2-119b). Executing the two-pass protocol on fresh04: apply k=1/0.66, then regenerate everything from the pano at true scale.

- **R-S2-119b amendment (2026-08-11C):** HUG_DRIFT_M 0.20 -> 0.30 by user ruling — the 0.2 was eyeballed against the un-normalized (0.66x) room; with the scene now in true meters: "now i know that the scene is actually miniature. i think 0.3 is reasonable." A good example of why eye-calibrated knobs must be re-read after normalization changes what the eye was looking at.

## R-S2-121 - NORMALIZED SCENES VERIFY, NEVER CRASH: THE SECOND-APPLY GUARD MET THE RUNNER (2026-08-11C)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: a re-run of the scale stage on a scene whose normalization was already applied. DECIDES: nothing - it verifies, and says how metric the scene now measures. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: the guard that prevents double-scaling killing every normalized scene's re-run instead.
- **FOUND by the two-pass regeneration itself:** fresh04's funnel re-ran at true scale, re-measured s=1.049 (doors 0.93-1.05 of typical - THE NORMALIZATION WORKED, metric within 5%), computed k=0.953, and fell into "REFUSING second apply" - exit 1, stage crash. The runner never passes --measure-only, so EVERY normalized scene crashes at scale on EVERY re-run: a guaranteed batch-night defect, found the only way this class ever is - by running.
- **FIXED at the source:** boot.scale_applied now routes the stage to the verification path automatically (scene_scale_verify.json, loud print of the verification s). The refusal stays beneath as the backstop for the one thing it must prevent - a second in-place apply. Stage is now idempotent for the runner.
- Resuming fresh04 from scale (shell onward re-derives on the metric scene).

- **R-S2-121 addendum:** the gate CAUGHT the first version of this fix - the verify path left the stage`s promised scene_scale.json stale and the no-op trap failed it, exactly as built to. Second version: a verification APPENDS to scene_scale.json (a `verifications` list under the intact apply evidence) and still writes the verify record. The trap stays at full strength; the stage keeps its promise on every path.

## R-S2-122 - J9 LEARNS FULL-vs-PARTIAL, AND THREE MEMORY-CHECKS ANSWERED (2026-08-12)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: J9`s same-product pools and the size-write they trigger; plus the user`s three canon-memory questions. DECIDES: which set members may share the product`s size. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: a fragment of a wardrobe wall lending its 6 cm "depth" to a real 85 cm wardrobe.
- **USER RULING (the obj_081 postmortem): ask J9 per member whether it is reasonably the FULL product or a PARTIAL piece of one.** Their own example, now in the prompt verbatim: a chair seen only from the top is still reasonably a whole chair (full); one door face of a wardrobe wall matches the look but is a PIECE (partial). BUILT: prompt + answer schema gain `coverage: full|partial`; partial members keep the SAME_PRODUCT relationship and their OWN measured box; the exemplar may only be a full member; a set with no full member gets no canonical size. Old caches lack the field and default to `full` (pre-ruling behavior, no silent change). NOTE: this deliberately un-parks a slice of PARKED #2 (J9 quality) by the user`s own investigation. Prompt change = new cache keys = affected pools re-judged.
- **SECOND RULING: wall items may sit INSIDE their wall** ("wall objects should be able to be behind the walls, like in-wall wardrobe"). fit_check no longer reports wall-plane depth for mount=wall items (floor/ceiling still reported); declip already never pushes a wall item off its plane.
- **MEMORY CHECKS, verified against code:**
  1. "If no asset fits, walk back to the pre-proposal state; nothing within margins = discard" - TRUE AS DESIGN, half-dormant in fact: fit_feedback writes exactly those verdicts (worst-axis >65% = rejection; canon rule 9) and shopping.py:165-179 consumes them (rejected swaps revert to out-items, rejected adds vanish) - but only when shopping RUNS AGAIN, and the automatic re-shop is deliberately unbuilt (PARKED #5, user`s own 08-11 ruling). ALSO: the guard measures asset-vs-proposed-box, so the wardrobe passed it - a wrong-but-fillable box is invisible to it.
  2. "propose_edits can merge boxes?" - NO, and the word "merge" in my earlier explanation was loose: its vocabulary is add / delete / swap only; the wardrobe was a SWAP with five out-items. No graph box was ever merged or edited; the flattening was J9`s size-write, upstream.
  3. "We never add anchor objects, or objects of a certain size" - NOT in code or canon: shopping`s own contract admits "real detections + accepted adds + swap-ins" to the anchor tier, and no size cap exists on adds or swap-ins. The guards that DO exist: proposal confidence, displacement measurement, box_source=estimated_prior stamp (which nothing downstream currently reads - the open invented-anchor gap, decision still with the user).

## R-S2-123 - CANON RULE 9 GETS ITS TEETH: TRULY-UNFITTING INVENTED ITEMS ARE NOT PLACED (2026-08-12)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: the fit set with each item`s ranked candidates. DECIDES: whether an invented item (add / swap-in) whose BEST candidate is still past the dry bar stands in the room at all. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: a hopeless stand-in board in the preview with its rejection politely filed for a re-shop that never runs.
- **USER RULING (worried about panel shopping before compose): "if something is really not fitting in the box by a large margin, i`d rather not have it. i thought we talked about this last time."** They HAD - canon rule 9, 2026-08-04, their own wardrobe ruling ("the wardrobe should be abandoned...") - but enforcement was deferred to fit_feedback`s walk-back verdicts, which only bite on a next shopping run that is deliberately unbuilt (PARKED #5). The rule existed; its teeth did not.
- **BUILT, at the placer:** fit_preview skips an add/swap-in whose best candidate`s worst-axis score exceeds fit_feedback.DRY_SCORE (0.65 - the SAME constant, imported, no new threshold). Skipped items are counted and named in fitted_preview.json `not_placed` and printed loudly. DETECTED objects still place best-effort with their least-bad candidate - the room really contains them, absence would be a worse lie; fit_feedback`s advisory split unchanged.
- Landed while compose was mid-run, upstream of the placer - stages load code fresh, so this pass already enforces it.

## R-S2-124 - THE SIZE BAR COVERS EVERY ITEM, AT THE BAR THE USER ALREADY RULED (2026-08-12)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: every fit-set item`s ranked candidates (assets judged at NATIVE size, 08-03B canon - no rescale-to-box). DECIDES: whether ANY item stands in the room when its best candidate is far from its box`s size. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: a door standing at 219% of its box because "the room really contains a door".
- **THE CORRECTION FIRST:** I told the user placement scales assets to the box. WRONG - shopping.py`s own header: "FIT = NATIVE SIZE ONLY (user rulings 08-03B): no rescale - a product is judged at the size it really is." The user caught it from memory, the run was stopped on their conditional instruction, and the R-S2-123 invented-only split was built on my misreading of what placement does.
- **USER RULING:** "all items need a bar... the point of the boxes is that they should be around that size. a single bed is sized as a single bed, a door is sized as a door not a barn. nothing will be exact due to assets, so slightly generous margins." And their second catch: "we discussed a lot of these previously - check the log."
- **THE LOG AGREES WITH THEM TWICE.** 2026-08-05C SR4c ruled THIS EXACT DESIGN at the sub tier: "best of the WHOLE shortlist over DRY 0.65 -> adds drop entirely, detections drop with a recorded complaint" - with calibration receipts (0.931 dropped; 0.538 survived flagged POOR_FIT). The anchor tier never inherited it because the sub rounds sat deferred in experiments/ (PARKED #4). No new number exists to choose: the bar is the ruled DRY 0.65.
- **BUILT:** fit_preview`s not-placed check now covers EVERY source (detected included), bar = fit_feedback.DRY_SCORE imported as SIZE_BAR (one knob). Dropped items counted and named in fitted_preview.json `not_placed`, printed loudly. On this scene`s own data the bar would have kept out the 219%-off door and the 1718%-off console that stood in earlier previews.
- Compose restarted from the top of the phase (early-stage caches make the redo cheap).

## R-S2-125 - FLAT AXES MEASURE IN CENTIMETERS: THE THIN-ITEM MASSACRE UNDONE (2026-08-12)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: native-size fit scoring (worst axis). DECIDES: how a near-zero box axis counts against a candidate. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: a 4 cm-thick door scored "100% wrong" against a 2 cm detection slab and vetoed on the axis that matters least.
- **THE FINDING (R-S2-124`s first full run):** 19 of 43 items not placed, and almost all of them FLAT - doors (near-perfect in height, killed by slab thickness), the 2 cm ceiling-disc box vs any real lamp (950%), the 2 cm rug box (4715%). Percentage deviation is meaningless on a wafer axis. USER: "percentage only doesn`t seem to be a reasonable metric... anything less than 15cm is considered flat, and we can be slightly generous."
- **BUILT (shopping.native_fit):** axes where the BOX is under FLAT_AXIS_M = 0.15 m score ABSOLUTELY - a miss within FLAT_TOL_M = 0.15 m is free, the excess counts in units of 0.15 so it stays on the ratio scale. Fat axes unchanged; the 0.65 bar unchanged; both constants named with the ruling.
- **VERIFIED PREDICTIONS stay honest:** the bench stays dead (best asset 1.11 m vs 0.58 m box - a FAT-axis 91%, the barn-door case); the fake "rug" stays dead (asset is not a rug); doors live or die on their real WIDTH now (the library`s 2.09 m double door vs a 0.82 m opening still fails, and should). Pillows were never bar-killed: subs_deferred by the limit-the-search-tree canon, sub rounds PARKED #4.
- Stale-metric walk-back verdicts (rejected swap_r1n2/swap_r3n1/add_r2n4, computed under the old scoring) cleared with the re-run so they cannot veto items the new metric accepts; fit_feedback regenerates them fresh at the loop`s end.

## R-S2-126 - SYNONYM ESCALATION FOR HOPELESS ITEMS: THE BENCH GETS TO TRY THE OTTOMAN AISLE (2026-08-12)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: items whose best candidate is past the DRY bar after the lexical aisles are searched. DECIDES: whether sibling aisles (via the existing batched label-mapper, now fed name + the judge`s description) can offer a fitting candidate before the item is abandoned. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: "nothing fits" declared after searching only the aisle the item`s name happened to spell.
- **USER:** the "bench" is really a bed-end bench / nightstand-class piece; "are we searching synonyms like we do in vocab?" - answer NO: vocab hunts synonym families, shopping stopped at the first lexical aisle hit, and the mapper only ever saw labels with NO lexical match (lift noise). RULING: "only escalate the empty item, search synonyms, if nothing fits then we abandon."
- **BUILT (shopping.py):** after the shortlist pass, items with best score > DRY_SCORE (or no candidates) go through ONE batched map_labels_agent call - each label enriched with the judge`s appearance description so the mapper knows what the thing IS, not just what detection called it. New aisles` candidates merge (uid-deduped, best-first, TOP_N cap), `escalated_categories` records the trail. Mapper failure degrades honestly: the bar stands. Costs one batched call per compose run, only when something is hopeless.
- The R-S2-124 not-placed rule is unchanged - it now fires only after the escalation, making its claim ("no candidate anywhere reasonable") true.

- **R-S2-126b (2026-08-12):** the bench exposed the escalation`s flaw on its first run - the plain label mapper, asked about a "bench", answers "bench": the aisle that just failed. New `retrieve2.map_alternatives_agent`: told the aisle FAILED ON SIZE, given description + measured box, forbidden from repeating searched aisles, asked for stand-in categories (the ottoman/footstool class). shopping`s escalation now calls it. First run`s receipts kept: 7 hopeless, only the 2 with nonsense lexical aisles gained; the 5 with correct-but-unfitting aisles (incl. the bench) got nothing from the old prompt.

- **R-S2-126c (2026-08-12): ESCALATION WITHDRAWN BY THE USER, same night.** First live run: a console table came back as a BED, a headboard as a MIRROR - size-plausible, style-judge-approved, categorically absurd. "the escalation is odd... please stop" / "this makes no sense." The mapper answers "what could stand in at this size" with no notion of functional identity, and the style judge then decorates the mistake. Cross-category substitution WAITS until it has a similarity design; hopeless items are honestly absent (R-S2-124). Machinery kept behind `--escalate`, DEFAULT OFF; both bad picks were run-state only (re-run restores). The bench question that motivated it stands open as a design item, not a mechanism.

## R-S2-127 - ESCALATION REDESIGNED BY THE USER: MEANING NOMINATES, SIZE DISPOSES, ONE AISLE AT A TIME (2026-08-12)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: items hopeless after their own aisles (best > DRY 0.65). DECIDES: whether a same-function stand-in category, nearest first, can furnish them - or they die honestly. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: a bed for a console table (the withdrawn 126b, which let SIZE nominate categories and let the style judge cross-shop a merged pool).
- **THE USER`S DESIGN, verbatim:** "bench -> other possible categories by function or by synonym, like stool etc, ordered by closeness, then we search those category using our normal routine... if not then we go down to the next term, maybe up to like 3 terms? then if nothing we kill it." Refined mid-build: "if we are using a LLM call to find synonyms, then we can just give it the categories we got" - the model answers with up to 3 ordered CATALOG categories directly (no lossy term-to-aisle hop), from a function-only question that never mentions size.
- **WHY THIS SHAPE IS SAFE where 126b was not:** meaning nominates the aisles; the walk is SEQUENTIAL nearest-first (a far category is consulted only after a near one FAILED the bar); each attempt is the normal one-aisle routine, so the style judge can never cross-shop; three categories, then honestly absent. Trail recorded per item (`escalation_trail`), the winning stand-in named (`substituted_as`) and shown on the viewer card as `standing in as "X"`.
- 126b/c machinery removed; the withdrawal note stands as history.

- **R-S2-128b (2026-08-12, user: "make sure we dont run stale shit"):** the LAST memory channel closed. fit_feedback verdicts now carry a METRIC FINGERPRINT (DRY + flat-axis + fit-tol constants); shopping ignores verdicts whose stamp differs from its own constants - loudly, and unstamped pre-guard files count as stale. With R-S2-128 (walk/pick choices validated against current candidates) and fresh scenes starting memoryless, every cross-run memory channel in the compose loop is now validated at the moment of use: choices die with the shopping that made them, verdicts die with the canon that scored them.

## R-S2-129 - A REPORT LINE KILLED THE FUNNEL: print_gap_stats ON A SCENE WITH NO FLOOR-ISH LIFTS (2026-08-12)
- **CONTRACT.** GETS: the lifted object list and the floor height, purely to PRINT the floor-gap acceptance numbers. DECIDES: nothing - it is a progress report inside pano_lift. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: exactly what happened - the print itself crashing the lift stage after the manifest was already written, so a scene DIED on a line whose only job was to describe it.
- **WHAT HAPPENED (fresh05, the first world after fresh04, run solo as the R-S2-115..127 validation):** the f47d4f9d living room lifted 5 detections, none with a floor-ish label (all wall/ceiling class: panel, picture, ceiling light, curtain). `lift_sweep.print_gap_stats` took `np.percentile` of that empty floor-ish array -> IndexError -> pano_lift exit 1 -> run_scene CRASH at `lift`, 6 stages in. scene_manifest_pano2c.json was ALREADY on disk and healthy.
- **FIX AT SOURCE, scene-agnostic:** both arrays in `print_gap_stats` (all-objects and floor-ish) now print `(none)` when empty - the SAME guard its sibling G3 stats block six lines above has had all along (`if len(flg):`, lift_sweep.py:322). No threshold moved, no behaviour beyond the print changed. Any sparse scene - and a 100-scene batch will contain several - would have died on this line.
- **NOT TOUCHED, deliberately:** fresh05's detection IS starved (48 raw detections vs fresh04's 477; no sofa/table/console found; eye-level views measure dim, mean 47 vs 90, contrast 25 vs 42). That is scene content for the morning report and the user's worlds-gate design question - not something to tune a threshold over mid-run (Rule #1). The run resumes with `--from lift` and whatever the funnel honestly finds is the result.

## R-S2-130 - THE PRIORS CALL CAME BACK WITHOUT JSON AND KILLED scale (2026-08-12)
- **CONTRACT (get_priors).** GETS: the scene's label list. DECIDES: nothing itself - it fetches the class-size priors the measurement uses; the DECISION (scale vs degrade-to-1.0) belongs to the MIN_N/MAX_SPREAD gate downstream. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: this - one flaky model reply crashing the whole funnel at stage `scale`, when the module's own docstring promises "DEGRADES CONSERVATIVELY rather than guessing."
- **WHAT HAPPENED (fresh05 resume, 6 min in):** `call_claude` returned exit 0 with a reply containing no `{` at all (empty or non-JSON stdout - the shape claude.exe produces on a transient hiccup; the reply was not recorded, which is part of why the guard now prints the head). `raw.find("{")` = -1, the slice was empty, json.loads raised, stage exit 1.
- **FIX AT SOURCE, scene-agnostic:** get_priors now retries the call ONCE on an unparseable reply, and on a second failure prints the reply head LOUDLY and returns EMPTY priors - zero measurements - which lands in the module's already-designed MIN_N degrade path (scale 1.0, evidence recorded, scene untouched). Nothing new is invented: parse failure now routes to the same honest exit weak evidence always had. Only a PARSED reply is cached, so a degraded scene re-asks on its next run instead of remembering the failure.
- No threshold moved. The gate (MIN_N 5, MAX_SPREAD 0.15) is untouched.

## R-S2-131 - THE EMPTY WORLD: A FUNNEL THAT ENDS AT ZERO OBJECTS NOW REFUSES INSTEAD OF SHIPPING A CONTRADICTION (2026-08-12)
- **CONTRACT (build_graph guard).** GETS: the f30 manifest. DECIDES: whether there is anything to build a graph FROM - zero objects means the stage's whole job is impossible, and it exits 2 (REFUSED, the established verdict run_scene and the fleet table already name) with the receipt: how many were filtered below the bar, how many recenter refuted. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: what fresh05 did without the guard - an empty graph flowing on, build_judged stamping a `judged` layer with zero nodes, and the gate contradicting itself ("promised layer `judged` and it is not in the graph" from the after-gate; "the file says 'judged' is newest" from scene_state) because the presence test reads an empty layer as absent while the stamp reads as written.
- **HOW fresh05 GOT THERE (the whole funnel, honest at every step):** 48 raw detections (fresh04: 477) -> 5 lifted -> recenter REFUTED 3 -> the last survivor (ceiling light, 0.278) fell below f30's 0.30 -> 0 objects. scale degraded to 1.0 exactly as designed (n=0). Every stage told the truth; the machinery only broke where an empty layer met the stamping contract.
- **WHY REFUSE RATHER THAN TEACH THE CHAIN EMPTINESS:** an empty scene through vote/judges/compose is 30 stages of untested zero-node territory that proves nothing and would crash-cascade (this session already fixed two empty-array crashes, R-S2-129/130, before the graph was even reached). "This world gave us nothing measurable" is a fleet-table row (`refused`), not a scene. The empty-layer-vs-stamp gate question technically remains, but with the guard no empty layer can ship, so it is superseded rather than open.
- fresh05's contradictory scene_graph.json + empty-docket judge caches from tonight were deleted before the re-run; the scene's terminal state is the REFUSAL with its receipt, and the world (f47d4f9d) is evidence for the user's worlds-gate design question: splat renders dim at eye level (brightness 47 vs fresh04's 90), detector starved, zero survivors.

## R-S2-132 - THE §4 TRAP CLOSED: --from/--until NOW SCOPE THE WHOLE CHAIN UNDER --phase all (2026-08-12)
- **CONTRACT (run_scene range handling).** GETS: --phase, --from, --until. DECIDES: which stages run. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: the trap the 08-26 handoff §4 names ("not yet fixed or logged as a defect; worth a refusal or a clearer semantic before batch nights") - `--from X --phase all` scoping only X's own table while every OTHER phase, EARLIER ONES INCLUDED, re-ran in full. It cost a full funnel re-run last night (fit_preview resume) and again tonight (build_graph resume re-ran all eleven intake stages, 200 s and a detect pass, to arrive where the scene already stood).
- **THE CLEARER SEMANTIC, taken from the two real uses:** under `--phase all` the named range is a position in the WHOLE chain - phases before --from's table are prerequisites and are SKIPPED (loud line says so), phases after --until's do not run, phases inside run whole. `--from lift` still means "resume mid-intake and finish the scene" (later phases were virgin both times it was used); `--from build_graph` now means what every caller meant: intake is DONE, go from the graph.
- **TWO REFUSALS ADDED with it:** (1) an explicit `--phase P` with a --from/--until stage that lives in a DIFFERENT table used to run P silently in full - now refused as the contradiction it is; (2) a typo'd --from/--until used to select every table in full (each `_range_for` silently returned None) and the run looked like a resume - now validated against the stage tables up front, run_fleet-style.
- Verified by dry-run: build_graph resume skips core; `--phase compose --from vote` refuses; `--from typo` names every table. `--phase <name>` alone is untouched.

## R-S2-133 - A DEGENERATE POLYGON FIT FOLDED SILENTLY AND CRASHED COMPOSE 26 STAGES LATER (2026-08-12)
- **CONTRACT (fold_polygon_into_shell acceptance).** GETS: the clean polygon the W4 trace produced. DECIDES: whether that polygon is structurally a room at all before it becomes the shell contract every consumer reads. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: fresh06 - the trace escaped the room (open door / splat leakage on an otherwise clean painterly bedroom) and "succeeded" with 3 segments, two of them 70 METERS long, ONE cardinal wall per axis; nothing validated it, the fold shipped it, 26 stages ran fine on top of it, and the first reader that counts planes (arch_walls.wall_axis_planes, >= 2 per axis by documented contract) crashed compose/propose_edits.
- **FIX AT SOURCE, structural not tuned:** the fold now refuses a polygon with < 2 axis-bearing wall segments per axis - a closed box-ish room cannot have one x-wall, so this is geometry, not a threshold. The raise lands in main()'s EXISTING degrade path: shell stays v1 4-plane, polygon_error recorded in room_shell.json, never a silent skip. fresh06's v1 walls are sane (4 axis walls), so the degraded shell is a correct room.
- **NOTE for the W4 recipe owners:** the interesting upstream question - why the trace escaped on a box room - is a fit-quality design matter, deliberately not touched tonight. The degrade path is the designed answer for exactly this case.
- fresh06 resumed `--from shell` (the R-S2-132 semantic's first live use: intake before shell skipped, everything after re-runs). THE BAR MOVES AGAIN: fresh07 is the next zero-intervention validation world.

## R-S2-134 - THE EYE WAS 1.6 RAW UNITS ABOVE THE FLOOR, AND TWO WORLDS WERE PHOTOGRAPHED FROM INSIDE THEIR CEILINGS (2026-08-12)
- **CONTRACT (pano_stitch eye placement).** GETS: the frame's floor/ceiling. DECIDES: the ONE standpoint the whole pipeline sees the room from - every crop, detection, lift and judge stimulus descends from this camera. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: fresh05 and fresh07 - "dud worlds" that were really dud PHOTOGRAPHS.
- **THE DEFECT, systematic and invisible per-scene:** the eye sat at floor + 1.6 RAW UNITS. Marble worlds arrive at arbitrary export scale (the reason scene_scale exists), and scale is unknown until AFTER stitch. fresh05's room is 1.63 raw tall -> the eye was 0.03 from the ceiling, inside the ceiling fixtures; fresh07's is 1.46 -> the eye was 0.14 ABOVE the ceiling, outside the room, seeing backfaces. Symptoms downstream: dim low-contrast views (brightness 47 vs fresh04's 90), 15/20 and 9/20 camera verifications, 48 and 8 raw detections, zero funnel survivors - and both worlds landed honest REFUSALS that blamed the WORLD.
- **FIX AT SOURCE, scale-invariant not tuned:** the eye height is now a FRACTION of the measured room height - EYE_FRAC = 1.6/2.8, the same 1.6 m-in-a-2.8 m-standard-room constant the pipeline already uses (scene_scale's prior), re-expressed so it cannot leave the room. A metric-scale room gets the identical camera as before; an off-scale room gets the same sightline geometry. No-ceiling frames keep the old absolute as fallback, loudly. New eye per scene: fresh04 1.458 (was 1.6), fresh05 0.932 (was 0.03-from-ceiling), fresh06 1.758, fresh07 0.833 (was outside the room). The face cache keys on the eye, so re-runs re-render automatically.
- **VERDICTS REOPENED:** fresh05's and fresh07's refusals were OUR defect, not their worlds' - both re-run from stitch. fresh05's R-S2-131 'dud world' framing is corrected by this entry; the refusal machinery worked exactly as designed, it was fed a bad photograph.

## R-S2-135 - THE PARALLEL-RUN EXPERIMENT: TWO PIPELINE SCENES + TWO GLTS RUNS SHARED THE MACHINE, AND WHAT IT COST (2026-08-12)
- **CONTRACT (the experiment).** GETS: two never-run scenes (fresh05 retry, fresh09) as concurrent run_scene processes, then two GLTS layout runs (fresh04, fresh06, --parallel 2) added on top. DECIDES: whether multi-scene execution crossfires - files, GPU, or the shared claude.exe subscription. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: the 2026-08-11 contamination folder (two writers, one output root) or a mid-run hard power-off.
- **STAGGER DESIGN:** scene B launched when scene A cleared its GPU-heavy intake, so A's judge phase (LLM) overlapped B's intake (GPU); GLTS joined only after B's detect. The five gpu_lock sites arbitrated the WSL renders; detect/stitch (unprotected) never ran twice at once by construction.
- **RESULT - NO CROSSFIRE:** fresh05 PASS (51/51 evidence, 30 placed/3 receipts, dry in 2) and fresh09 PASS (first fully COLLIDERLESS world end-to-end, 54/54 evidence) while GLTS ground beside them. Zero file collisions (per-scene dirs + scene_lock), zero clock-over-lock samples and zero VRAM-over-11GB samples across the whole night (monitors on gpu_watch.csv, 1 Hz), no judge failures attributable to rate-limit contention.
- **WHAT PARALLELISM COSTS, measured:** wall-clock per scene stretches. fresh09 (a simple office) took 85 min in company vs fresh08's 78 min SOLO for a bigger bedroom; GLTS under three-way contention paced far below its solo baseline (~55-63 min/scene solo on 2026-08-11; still running past 2.5 h for two scenes tonight, sequential model calls being the bottleneck). The subscription is ONE lane: everything shares it, nothing corrupts, everything slows.
- **TWO OPERATIONAL FINDINGS FOR FLEET NIGHTS:** (1) background tool-shell tasks were mass-killed mid-run (~09:15) by the shell job-object reaping this project already documented for servers - long runs must launch DETACHED (WMI Win32_Process.Create, the watch_gpu pattern); all three workloads resumed with zero lost state (scene locks broke stale, caches held, GLTS restarted per its own design - though GLTS has NO mid-run resume, so its first hour was repaid in full). (2) run_fleet remains sequential by design; tonight's evidence says a two-lane fleet (stagger by phase) is safe on this machine, but that is a DESIGN CHANGE for the user to rule on, not tonight's.
- **GLTS FINAL NUMBERS (user: "interesting how long this took. log these"):** fresh04 ok in 185.9 min / 280 model calls; fresh06 ok in 192.5 min / 279 calls; 2/2 ok, layout-only (steps 0-13, no Blender). Baselines for contrast: GLTS solo on 2026-08-11 was ~54-63 min / 138-172 calls per scene, so two-at-once under a shared subscription roughly TRIPLED wall time and the call counts nearly doubled the solo runs (MCTS spends more calls on these busier rooms). Our own pipeline's numbers the same night: fresh08 FULL 46-stage run, 78 min, zero intervention; fresh05 graph+compose 57 min. The July one-scene comparison (156 calls/~87 min vs ours 19/2.5 min, COMPARISON.html) still understates the gap at fleet scale: GLTS restarted from zero after the 09:15 kill (no mid-run resume), while both our scenes resumed mid-stage with nothing lost.

Same shared px-per-metre as the drawn plans, room outline overlaid, placed/not-placed/failed counts from fitted_preview.json in the caption.

## R-S2-138 - GLTS GETS ITS COMPOSED-SCENE RENDER TOO, WITHOUT BLENDER (2026-08-12)
- **CONTRACT (render_glts_composed).** GETS: GLTS's own outputs (13/14 layouts, 11_retrieved_results uids) + the local objathor store. DECIDES: nothing - it re-executes GLTS's OWN step-15 assembly recipe and renders. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: our interpretation silently replacing theirs - which is why the recipe is transcribed line-for-line from their blender_placement.py: verts [x,-z,y], Z-rotation by (yRotOffset + [180,90,0,270][orientation]) CCW (checked against Blender Matrix.Rotation), x/y exact scale with z = mean(x,y factors) capped at 2x the exact z fit, bottom-center anchored at location (z=0 floor, parent's scaled height for small objects), small objects only on parents named table/desk/stand/cabinet (their rule, verbatim).
- **WHY IT WORKS WITHOUT BLENDER:** everything step 15 needs already exists after step 13/14 - the layout carries orientation, step 11 carries the asset uids, and all 56 asset pickles (33+23) are in our local objathor store. Blender adds only the Cycles image (lighting rig, floor texture), not placement. Colors come from the albedo textures sampled at the UVs; both methods' composed renders go through ONE painter rasterizer at ONE shared scale, so neither side can be prettier by construction.
- **CAPTION HONESTY:** the figure says it is our re-execution of their recipe, not their Blender render, and lists anything skipped.
- **REGENERATED:** out/comparison_20260812T222227Z.{json,html} - 7 figures per scene, 7/7 embedded (fresh04 + fresh06).

## R-S2-140 - SCALE GETS TWO TIERS: THE ROOM VOTES, AND ARCHITECTURE DECIDES WHEN FURNITURE ARGUES (2026-08-12)
- **CONTRACT (scene_scale, updated).** GETS: the f30 manifest rulers + the frame floor/ceiling. DECIDES: scale_to_meters. A MISTAKE LOOKS LIKE: the 08-12 overnight - fresh05 measured 0.64 on SEVEN agreeing doors and lost it to the spread gate by 0.0016 (rel-MAD 0.1516 vs 0.15), fresh08 measured a two-camp split (doors ~0.66 vs low-furniture ~0.30, median 0.447, spread 0.32) - and BOTH shipped raw with 1.6-1.7 m ceilings inside PASSes.
- **USER RULING (verbatim intent): "bias doors and ceiling; if the spread is big we take doors and ceiling height as the reference."** Two changes, no existing threshold moved:
  1. THE ROOM VOTES: one new ruler per scene - measured ceiling height vs ROOM_TYPICAL_M = 2.8 (the SAME standard-room constant the stitch eye fraction is built on). One row among n, no special weight.
  2. ARCH REFERENCE TIER: when consensus fails MAX_SPREAD, the median of doors+ceiling alone decides, gated by the same MAX_SPREAD on their own agreement and ARCH_MIN_N = 3 of them present. Doors and ceilings are standardized and style-immune; furniture can be low-profile (modern rooms) or occlusion-truncated. Arch stats are recorded on EVERY run as a cross-check; scene_scale.json names the deciding tier.
- **MEASURE-ONLY VERIFICATION, all six scenes:** fresh05 consensus now PASSES on its own (s=0.639, the room ruler tightened the cluster) -> ceiling 2.55 m once applied. fresh08 consensus honestly fails (0.33) -> arch tier decides s=0.643 (3 doors + room, rel-MAD 0.069) -> ceiling 2.69 m once applied. fresh04/06/07 verify at 0.99-1.02 (still metric, unaffected). fresh09 WATCH ITEM: post-normalization doors read 1.087 vs its ceiling 0.832 - verification only, recorded, no action; belongs in the viewer walk.
- **NOT DONE YET:** the actual apply + two-pass re-runs of fresh05 and fresh08 (geometry mutates, then the chain re-runs from stitch). Awaiting the user go - long detached runs, clock-lock protocol.
